He may have been right, but the penalty didn’t come and his team went without a touchdown for the second straight week.

Klubnik started the second half while Smith was being evaluated for a concussion. He was cleared and returned for one drive before leaving with an ankle injury. He was ruled out for good in the fourth quarter and Glenn will provide more information about his availability for Week 11’s road date with the Chargers.

That game will have significant implications on the draft order. The same is true of the Bills’ game against the Dolphins, although Buffalo will be more interested in what it means for their Super Bowl pursuit than its impact on the top of April’s selection process.

#2752. High school football: Guided by Zehr at quarterback, Lowville routs Bishop Ludden-Grimes/SAS to claim second consecutive Section 3 crown

SYRACUSE — Toran Zehr and his Lowville football teammates undertook a daunting and challenging mission entering this season to deliver another sectional title.

On Saturday, the Red Raiders achieved just this, securing a repeat championship in emphatic fashion.

In what proved to be a historic day, top-seeded Lowville dominated on both sides of the ball en route to recording a 43-14 rout of third-seeded Bishop Ludden-Grimes/SAS in the Class C final on Saturday with many of their faithful in attendance at the JMA Wireless Dome.

With the triumph, the Red Raiders won their second consecutive Section 3 championship, a program first, improving to 10-1 on season. Last year, they claimed the sectional crown in Class D.

"Hey man, it feels great," Zehr said. "Especially being an underclassman and getting to do this twice. And it's great, these seniors are crazy athletes, they all come down here, and every game they're ready to perform their best."

"I'm having trouble putting it into words right now," Lowville senior running back Cole Zubrzycki said. "Going to the Dome as a team and winning back to back, it's something that you dream about."

Zehr, a sophomore quarterback, threw for a touchdown in the first half and went on to run for a pair of scores to help spark Lowville.

Displaying balance they've generated throughout the season, Zubrzycki, Ethan Myers and Caleb Farney each ran for a touchdown for the Red Raiders.

"We've got awesome chemistry," Zubrzycki said. "We like to always have a little bit of competition, but it's always friendly because that's what we've to do. We push each other to get better and get it done."

"It's unbelievable," Myers, who is also a senior, said. "The first team in school history to win back-to-back sectional championships, so it's really awesome."

Lowville also successfully shut down a speedy Gaelic Knights team for most of the day, delivering swarming defensive play.

"This is special," Lowville coach Josh Coffman said. "This is a special group and they really showed that today. It's just awesome what they've been able to achieve."

Slade Nortz would provide his team with an early lift by intercepting a pass in the first quarter for the Red Raiders.

Lowville broke through on their second possession of the game as Zubrzycki hauled in a 66-yard catch, speeding down the right sideline from midfield to the Bishop Ludden-Grimes/SAS 17-yard line.

Then with Lowville facing a fourth-and-long, Zehr connected with Carson Kempney, who was wide open in the left side of the end zone and hauled in an 18-yard scoring catch to cap a 90-yard drive in nine plays. After Nolan Roes ran in the conversion run the Red Raiders led 8-0 just six seconds into the second quarter.

"That was good, especially since we had somewhat of a slow start in the first quarter," Zehr said. "I think just getting that really lifted us up."

Following a Bishop Ludden-Grimes/SAS punt, the Red Raiders methodically moved the ball down the field in generating a 10-play drive finished off by a one-yard scoring surge from Zubrzycki to extend the lead to 14-0.

Lowville made their last possession of the first half count as well as Zehr scored on a 21-yard scamper on a keeper to the left side, lunging into the end zone. Zehr then ran in the conversion attempt to boost the advantage to 22-0 with 1:13 left in the half.

"Toran is one of those guys who always makes plays when you need one," Zubrzycki said. "He's just a great athlete, I trust him with the ball, any point in the game, any situation, I always trust him with the ball."

"It was a great team effort, I think our blocking was great," Zehr said. "And the defense played great."

The Red Raiders picked up where they left off as they drove 65 yards in 11 plays, capped by a 16-yard touchdown run from Zehr to extend the advantage to 29-0 with 7:41 left in the third quarter.

"Cole and Ethan have helped me a lot through these two years, mentoring me and making sure I'm doing the right stuff — and really challenging me," Zehr said.

"We've been so successful with Cole and Ethan and Caleb running the ball, we've been kind of hesitant with Toran who's a pretty important part of the team," Coffman said. "He doesn't play any defense for us and he's a tremendous defender and a great tackler. We haven't really used him in the running game nearly as much as he's capable of, but he did really well for us."

Myers would add a 31-yard touchdown run in the fourth quarter to push the lead to 36-0.

After Bishop Ludden-Grimes/SAS broke through for a touchdown on a 44-yard touchdown throw from Taveon Sledge to J'Shon Goolsby, Farney later scored on a six-yard run for Lowville boost the lead to 43-7.

Lowville, which is ranked No. 5 in the state, competed in its second straight sectional final, also a first in program history, and also won its 10th straight game this year.

"It feels great, we're just really trying to represent Lowville at our best," Zehr said. "I think we did a good job, obviously it's really cool to do something for the first time in the school's history."

"For a lot of us right now it's play or go home," Zubrzycki said. "So we came in and played our hearts out, we didn't want to go home without getting this done."

Farney, who plays at linebacker, said: "It's amazing, we worked all year for it. We put in a lot of time and effort to put it all together. We have a lot of natural athletes, but it took a lot work to really grow as a team."

Bishop Ludden-Grimes/SAS, which won on the road to defeat General Brown in a semifinal last Friday, finishes its season at 10-1.

"With their offense, we wanted to keep them in front of us, no big plays," Coffman said of Bishop Ludden-Grimes/SAS. "Tackle and make them go the length of the field and I think we did that well."

"We've got a great defense, a lot of athletic guys who play so well together," Farney said. "We never give up, we just keep pushing and keep improving."

With the win, Lowville has now extended its mission this season as it will move on to play the Section 4 champion in a state quarterfinal at noon next Saturday at Cicero-North Syracuse High School.

"It's just a special group," Coffman said. "They're kind of like my kind of guys, they're hard-nosed, they're tough, they come to work each day and sometimes you can't even tell if they're excited to be there. They just go, they have that kind of lunch box mentality and as a staff we love coaching them every single day."

Last year, the Red Raiders defeated Dolgeville to win the Class C sectional title before losing in a state quarterfinal to Tioga of Section 4.

"With Section 4, it will be our fourth time going down there and playing them," Coffman said. "Every time it's the same thing, they play a physical brand of football and it's just getting our kids to believe. They've got to just believe that we can get stops, you've got to get forward, you've got to get off blocks and all those things, and offensively it's all about execution and keeping our offense on the field."

#2751. New York Jets vs Buffalo Bills: Bills Blast Execrable Jets

In the New York Jets' 10th game of the 2026 season, on a breezy, chilly November day in East Rutherford, New Jersey, the Buffalo Bills blew out the Jets, 31 – 12.

The game opened with the Jets on defense. The Bills advanced past midfield before they were forced to punt. The Jets then went nowhere and were forced to punt out of their own end zone.

The Bills then went on a long, grinding drive ending in a Josh Allen quarterback sneak for a touchdown and a 7 – 0 Bills lead late in the first quarter.

The first quarter ended with the score still Bills 7, Jets 0. The Jets had the ball in Bills territory. On third down and 14 the Jets did a Jets thing, throwing well short of the sticks to Mason Taylor. They settled for a short field goal to make the score 7 – 3 in favor of the Bills. Shades of last week against the Chiefs when the Jets constantly settled for field goals against a team they knew would score often against them. Trying to make the score respectable rather than trying to win.

The Bills came right back with a scoring drive of their own. They narrowly missed a touchdown when James Cook dropped a perfect pass that likely would have ended in Cook scoring. Instead the Bills settled for a field goal and a 10 – 3 lead midway through the second quarter.

Neither team got anything going again on offense until the Jets put together a drive featuring some nice Geno Smith to Garrett Wilson connections late in the quarter. The drive stalled inside the Bills 20 yard line and the Jets settled for another field goal with less than 20 seconds left in the half. The Jets went into the locker room trailing the Bills 10 – 6.

The Jets opened the third quarter on offense. Geno Smith was on the sideline with an injury he incurred at he end of the first half. Cade Klubnik made his NFL debut at quarterback for the Jets. Klubnik put together a nice drive to open the second half, using his legs to run for a couple of first downs and finding Garrett Wilson for a long completion. Unfortunately, the drive stalled deep in Bills territory. The Jets had to again settle for a field goal, making the score Bills 10, Jets 9.

The Jets momentum was fleeting. The Bills answered with a quick touchdown drive highlighted by a long catch and run by Dalton Kincaid. Josh Allen finished the drive by running it into the end zone and the Bills led 17 – 9.

On the ensuing Jets drive Cade Klubnik exited and Geno Smith returned. Smith led the Jets on another drive that stalled deep in Bills territory. Jason Sanders hit his fourth field goal of the game, making the score 17 – 12 in favor of the Bills late in the third quarter.

Again, the Jets momentum was fleeting. The Bills quickly struck back. James Cook ripped a 22 yard run, then Josh Allen hit D.J. Moore on a 45 yard bomb for a touchdown. Two plays, less than a minute and the Bills were in the end zone. The Bills were pulling away at 24 – 12 late in the third quarter.

Cade Klubnik re-entered the game at this point, replacing an injured Geno Smith. Klubnik quickly threw his first NFL interception, but on a wild play the Jets got the ball back as Jeremy Ruckert forced a fumble and the Jets recovered the ball. The drive then went nowhere and the Jets were forced to punt.

The fourth quarter opened with the Bills on offense deep in their own territory. Josh Allen threw a long pass that was intercepted by Azareye'h Thomas and the Jets had some life with 13 minutes remaining in the game. Unfortunately, Thomas was injured on the play. It would take a big comeback by the rookie Cade Klubnik and company, but the Jets had a little life.

It didn't last. The two teams exchanged drives going nowhere. Then the Jets put together a nice drive, getting deep in Bills territory before a hook and ladder play went awry. Garrett Wilson fumbled the lateral as he was getting tackled and the Bills recovered. On the next play James Cook broke a run for a 74 yard touchdown as the Bills put the final nail in the Jets coffin. The Bills led 31 – 12 with just under five minutes left in the game. The game ended a few minutes later without another score.

The loss brought the Jets' record to 2-8. One more loss and the Jets will have their 11th straight losing season to go along with their 16th straight season without the playoffs. Aaron Glenn is now 0-9 in the AFC East and 5-22 overall.

The Jets are on the road the next two weeks against the Los Angeles Chargers and the Miami Dolphins. The weaker opponents give the Jets a shot at a victory or two before they get their bye week in Week 13. However, the Jets are in no position to take anything for granted against any opponent. We'll see what kind of fight the team still has in them over the next couple of weeks.

#2750. Top Wall Street analysts are bullish on these 3 stocks for the long term

Major indexes were volatile this week due to earnings from artificial intelligence companies, key economic releases, and the scandal involving Nvidia former CEO Jensen Huang. While short-term fluctuations could weigh on investor sentiment, it is prudent to maintain a long-term investment horizon.

Investors looking for attractive long-term picks can track the ratings of top Wall Street analysts and gain key insights from their in-depth research on the macro and micro factors impacting stocks.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Cybersecurity company Palo Alto Networks will announce its fiscal first-quarter earnings on Nov. 18. Migration to the cloud and the rapid adoption of AI have increased demand for cybersecurity solutions provided by Palo Alto and its peers. TipRanks' KPIs tool for PANW shows the rise in the company's subscription revenue over the years, driven by the adoption of its AI and security offerings and contributions from recent acquisitions.

Ahead of the upcoming earnings, Oppenheimer analyst Ittai Kidron reiterated a buy rating on Palo Alto stock with a price target of $450. The five-star analyst said that his checks indicate upside potential from CyberArk and Chronosphere acquisitions and strength in the company's core businesses.

Specifically, Kidron expects Palo Alto's Q1 FY27 next-generation security annualized recurring revenue to be about two percentage points above the midpoint of the guidance range and consensus, or $9.55 billion. He also sees nearly 1 to 1.5 percentage points of revenue upside compared to the guidance and consensus of $3.305 billion. Kidron expects management to remain conservative with its FY27 guidance and raise it by no more than the size of the quarterly beat.

Additionally, Kidron highlighted that partner checks indicated above-plan performance in the quarter, driven by firewall-attached subscriptions and strength in Prisma SASE and XSIAM offerings. The analyst also expects results to gain from CyberArk cross-selling and better-than-expected performance of Chronosphere as OpenAI completes its migration.

"While Prisma AIRS crossed $100M in ARR last quarter, the fastest product ramp in company history, partners still view CY27 as the first real revenue year," said Kidron.

Kidron ranks No. 638 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 55% of the time, delivering an average return of 10.5%. See Palo Alto Technical Analysis on TipRanks.

Next on this week's list is database software company MongoDB. Recently, BTIG analyst Gray Powell initiated coverage of MDB stock with a buy rating and a price target of $417. Powell estimates that currently the size of the company's core addressable market is nearly $15 billion, with several tailwinds to drive more than 20% revenue growth over the next few years.

Beyond its core document and vector businesses, Powell highlighted that MongoDB has exposure to about $72 billion of adjacent markets, projected to grow to about $121 billion over four years. With even modest share gains in adjacent markets, the five-star analyst believes that MongoDB can grow revenue at about a 24% annual rate over the next three years, above management's growth target of more than 20%.

Contrary to the bearish narrative, Powell views AI as an emerging tailwind for MongoDB, with application modernization creating new workloads while making migrations faster and cheaper. He added that interviews with eight contacts were generally constructive and indicated faster legacy migrations, growing non-relational adoption, and early traction in Voyage AI and Vector Search offerings.

Additionally, Powell sees upside to Atlas growth, driven by new products. "We see a meaningful product cycle led by MongoDB 9.0 and AMP, with Agent Engine and Atlas Infinite adding upside over time," said the analyst.

Powell ranks No. 480 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 57% of the time, delivering an average return of 13%. See MongoDB Technical Analysis on TipRanks.

Membership-only warehouse chain Costco Wholesale recently announced a special cash dividend of $30 per share, payable on Dec. 4. The company also announced that its board has reauthorized a stock repurchase program of up to $4 billion, which will expire in November 2030 and replaces the current $4 billion program that is set to end in January 2027.

Following the announcement, Oppenheimer analyst Rupesh Parikh reiterated a buy rating on COST stock with a price target of $1,160. The five-star analyst said that the special dividend news is one of the key catalysts that he has been waiting for since Costco was added to Oppenheimer's top pick list in November 2025.

Parikh highlighted that the special dividend the company announced is significantly above his estimate of about $20 per share. The analyst expects the special dividend to reduce interest income substantially.

"COST remains a top pick for us, and we still see the potential for a stock split down the road, which could represent another positive catalyst for shares, in our view," said Parikh.

Overall, Parikh remains bullish on Costco's long-term prospects, backed by its value proposition, leading competitive position, and a solid track record of shareholder returns. The analyst also noted the company's strong management team and the ability to sustain top- and bottom-line growth even in a competitive retail backdrop.

#2749. Game Preview: San Antonio Spurs at Utah Jazz

The Utah Jazz have responded to the hype they got in the off season by winning one of their first 13 games. Injuries to their two best players have a lot to do with that, but they've been playing predictably and are struggling to make outside shots. Being small with four perimeter players and a big with no offensive bag has made it harder to bend opposing defenses, as a result, ensuring that they stay in a transition game more than they like.

Still, it would be unwise to think they will roll over quickly. Flawed as they are, they can creep back in through the door if the San Antonio Spurs are playing with their food. Even if a team has a basement record, sometimes they show up stronger than the numbers because they are desperate to get back in the win column.

The Spurs will head into Delta Center on an 11-game win streak, tied for their sixth-longest in franchise history. The streak is long enough that every team in the NBA is well aware, adding a higher level of intrigue to the game. This will be the third matchup between these teams this season.

Spurs Injuries: Tobias Harris — out (left calf strain); Jayden Quaintance — out (right knee surgery); Maliq Brown — out (G League); Ja'Kobi Gillespie — out (G League)

Jazz Injuries: Lauri Markkanen — out (right hand fracture); Jaren Jackson Jr. — out (right knee); Jaxson Hayes — out (left knee strain); Jusuf Nurkić — out (right foot); Trey Alexander — out (G League); Kobe Bufkin — out (G League); Harrison Ingram — out (G League).

Darryn Peterson is a rookie, and he has the goods. He's elusive on the dribble, mostly getting to work through screen rolls, with his preferred spots being the paint non-restricted area and at mid-range. He and Keyonte George should carry the bulk of the scoring load, so the Spurs will have to stay on their hips, and try to force them to attack going left, which is the off-hand of both players.

The Spurs starting lineup ties for the fifth-most used in the NBA, and it has been the most-efficient high-usage rotation, logging an impressive 84.6 defensive rating. It shouldn't be a surprise if this lineup is the one creating the most separation on the scoreboard since it has the most down-hill attackers who can quickly turn a miss into transition.

Even if the Spurs' transition numbers (55.2 percentile) aren't the best, they get out in open court so much that it wears out the opponents.

Victor Wembanyama is averaging 24 points, 12.5 rebounds and five assists in two games versus the Jazz this season, while making 78.6 of his shots within 10 feet. He may be in for a 40-point night since he will have some energy saved up from not having to go hard guarding his man, which could be Mo Bamba and some smaller players.

Julian Champagnie's role has changed a bit as a reserve this season, but his impact has been greater, being a top-notch release valve logging career high in efficiency by a significant margin (66.0 EFG percentage). He is averaging 42.2 percent from 3-point range for the year, and 44.4 percent against the Jazz. Notably, Champagnie is a feature in the third-most used Spurs' lineup, which is him with the starters in Devin Vassell's place. Expect Jazz defenders to lock-and-trail well when he changes spots in the half-court, but the schemes will be programmed to take away the on-ball threats. Champagnie will have the chance to feast because of that.

Since the level of competition is lower level, perhaps this is a game in which Carter Bryant will shine. His career high was 17 points last year, and even with his struggles this season, that number is beatable since the Jazz can't guard a parked car.

#2748. Vikings pummel Packers 30-10 in Kirk Cousins' return

The Vikings defense did what the Vikings defense does, but they also got some help from their offense and special teams on Sunday.

Minnesota torched Green Bay 30-10, winning in Kirk Cousins’ first game back in a Vikings uniform. It is the most points the Vikings have scored since a 39-22 shellacking of the Packers in Week 1.

The Vikings ended a two-game losing streak and moved to 7-2, while the Packers dropped to 6-4.

With Kyler Murray out and Carson Wentz ineffective last week, Minnesota brought back Cousins in a trade-deadline deal with the Vikings last week. Cousins previously spent 2018-23 with the team. He went 21-of-31 for 207 yards with two touchdowns and an interception, and the Vikings had 312 yards.

His touchdowns covered 38 yards to Myles Price and 20 yards to Gavin Bartholomew. The touchdown to Price came on third-and-23 and Cousins also converted a third-and-15 on a 24-yard throw to Tai Felton to the Green Bay 1 that set up Aaron Jones’ touchdown run.

Justin Jefferson caught nine passes for 74 yards and Jordan Mason had 13 carries for 51 yards.

The Packers had an interception of Cousins overturned by replay when Cousins was ruled down for a sack before releasing the ball.

The Vikings defense dominated, holding the Packers to 220 yards, forcing three turnovers and forcing eight sacks, including two by Levi Drake Rodriguez. The Packers also lost a fumble by J. Michael Sturdivant on a kickoff.

Trey Smack missed another field goal, a 53-yarder.

Jordan Love was 17-of-27 for 220 yards with a touchdown and an interception. Matthew Golden had five catches for 70 yards and three drops. Kaleb Johnson had 10 carries for 42 yards.

#2747. London City's 'bravery' paying off in top three pursuit

After 10 games in the Women's Super League season, London City Lionesses have shown they are capable of finishing inside the top three.

Their narrow 2-1 defeat by Manchester City two weeks ago provided snippets of their potential but a 1-1 draw at the Emirates against an improved Arsenal on Sunday confirmed their credentials.

Eder Maestre's side sit third in the table - the last qualification spot for next season's Women's Champions League - and are six points above Arsenal.

It is a strong position to be in just shy of the halfway stage having already faced big hitters Arsenal, Manchester City and Manchester United.

Current leaders Chelsea await in December but, for now, London City's progress is moving at a pace that looks set to challenge the usual suspects.

Find interviews and extra content on the BBC Women's Football Weekly feed

A sold-out away end helped fill out the 32,000-strong crowd at the Emirates on Sunday as many hoped for an end-to-end encounter.

They were not disappointed as fearless London City showed bravery against an Arsenal side who played with a spark following their 1-1 draw with Barcelona.

The visitors were helped out by Arsenal's wastefulness - the Gunners had 17 shots and only converted one - but London City caused plenty of problems of their own.

Maestre said after their defeat by Manchester City that their opponents showed more experience and know-how but against Arsenal, his side matured.

They were under pressure early on and fell a goal down thanks to Frida Maanum's opener but it took just 98 seconds for Izzy Goodwin to equalise for London City.

That killed Arsenal's momentum and when the pressure increased again from the hosts in the second half, goalkeeper and player of the match Mary Earps stepped up to make crucial saves.

"The performance was there. We had the chances to score and get a better result but Arsenal had chances too. The game was very open," said Maestre.

"The way we faced and challenged every circumstance in the game shows we are here, and able to play with that mentality in these big games.

"We came here to take risks. Our energy was good. We were brave and weren't scared about the context or the opponent.

"We tried to play as if we were at home. I always want to feel that my teams are brave and go for the win."

That bravery showed as Alexia Putellas came close twice with long-range efforts, Goodwin could have been onside when she was played in behind later on.

London City hit the crossbar through substitute Delphine Cascarino and had 14 shots compared to Arsenal's 17, including four inside the box.

Arsenal are still yet to win at home this season in the WSL but this was still an impressive showing from London City, who came to the Emirates to play.

But while they were willing to take risks, they were smarter than they were against Manchester City, choosing when to press aggressively and when to hold off.

"We tried to match the pace of the ball and not expose ourselves as much to the players that Arsenal have," added Maestre.

"We were trying to mix the moments we were more clinical in our press. We made some adjustments at half-time and were much better at recognising those."

London City are backed by US billionaire Michele Kang and that has made a big difference in the transfer market.

They recruited superstars Alexia Putellas, Mary Earps, Mapi Leon and Kadidiatou Diani in the summer.

Such is their depth that high-flying Spaniard Lucia Corrales, the dangerous Cascarino and England international Freya Godfrey came off the bench.

Those individual talents have made a difference and on Sunday it was goalkeeper Earps who took the plaudits, claiming the player of the match award.

She made crucial saves late on as Arsenal pushed ferociously for a winner, tipping Maanum's effort onto the crossbar and blocking her shot from close range.

Earps had to bide her time for the number one shirt after making the switch from Paris St-Germain this summer but has started the past three WSL games.

"Mary is a very strong character and is much more than the things we see in terms of the performance," said Maestre.

"She has experience, and energy that you can bring to every part in the process and not just the things we see in the matches.

"I think the energy that she brings to the position is massive and has a massive impact. I'm very pleased with her."

Watching on in the stands was England boss Sarina Wiegman who will have been pleased with the display from goalscorer Goodwin.

The 23-year-old got the better of England captain Leah Williamson when she fooled her with quick feet and fired the ball into the back of the net, making it 1-1.

She is proof that competition for places in the London City starting XI is pushing players on and that their depth is improving each week.

"I think Izzy is becoming a more mature player. She has the inner competition and the signings sometimes push you to bring 100% every time," added Maestre.

"She is committed to keep pushing and, with the performances she is having, why not [earn an England call-up]?

"Izzy is moving forward in the ways she can take advantage and that commitment is the way she is reaching those performances. I'm very proud of her."

London City's new training centre 'better than most Premier League clubs' - Kang

#2746. Patrick Mahomes throws five TDs, Chiefs beat Falcons 35-13

Patrick Mahomes was at his best on Sunday, throwing five touchdowns in a single game for the first time since 2022 to beat the Falcons 35-13.

Mahomes finished the game 22-of-35 passing for 348 yards with five touchdowns and no interceptions — good for a 141.2 passer rating.

He got things going with a 12-yard touchdown to running back Emmett Johnson to cap the team's first possession. While the Chiefs struggled to get much going for the rest of the first half, Mahomes hit newcomer Josh Downs for an 18-yard score with 18 seconds left in the second period to push Kansas City's lead to 14-3 at halftime.

Mahomes went on to pad the lead in the second half, with rookie Cyrus Allen catching a 39-yard deep pass down the left sideline for his first career touchdown. Then Johnson caught a 6-yard score with 9:55 left in the fourth quarter to make the score 28-3.

The Falcons made things interesting with a quick touchdown drive and an onside kick recovery — the first in 23 attempts all season across the NFL.

But Mahomes then fired a 43-yard deep ball to receiver Andrew Armstrong for a proverbial kill shot, putting the Chiefs up 35-13.

Mahomes has now thrown for five touchdowns seven times in his career. It's the fifth time he's accomplished that with no interceptions. Only Drew Brees, Tom Brady, and Peyton Manning have more games with five passing TDs in NFL history.

Filling in for the injured Kenneth Walker, Johnson was one of the stars on offense, finishing with 93 yards on 19 carries, plus six receptions for 70 yards with two TDs.

Downs, who just arrived via trade earlier this week, contributed with 53 yards on four catches with a touchdown. He also served as the team's punt returner.

The Falcons did not get much going offensively throughout the first three quarters before piling up yards in the fourth. Michael Penix Jr. finished 23-of-43 for 286 yards with an interception.. Drake London had six catches for 109 yards. He was checked for a concussion late in the contest and was announced as questionable to return. Bijan Robinson had 71 yards on 16 carries, plus four catches for 6 yards.

Now at 7-2, the Chiefs will host the Cardinals next week before a Thanksgiving showdown against the Bills.

At 3-7, the Falcons will be on their bye in Week 11 as they look to continue to contend for the NFC South.

#2745. Open Thread: Steve Kerr loves returning to San Antonio

Before Friday night's Emirates Cup competition, Golden State Warrior head coach Steve Kerr was asked by Clutch Point's Hector Ledesma if he still feels love from Spurs fans when he comes back to the Alamo City.

"I love coming back here. Went to Rosario's last night, that's my spot. I get such a great reception from fans here. If I'm waking around town or people at the hotel, employees, people saying, 'I'm a Spurs fan,' and thanking us for the years back then and that sort of thing. And then obviously coming into this arena is special. Just thinking about my last season, which was actually, I think, the first, the ninaugural season of this building. And so, it's one of my favorite stops on the tour coming back here and seeing a lot of old friends and feeling the love in San Antonio."

Steve Kerr came to San Antonio in 1999 after winning three titles with the Chicago Bulls. He won his fourth consecutive ring as the Spurs claimed their first NBA Championship. Kerr was traded after three seasons with the Silver & Black, but returned a year later.

The 2002-2003 season turned out to be his last, Kerr's four second-half three-pointers in Game 6 of the Western Conference Finals helped the Spurs eliminate the Dallas Mavericks. He picked up his fifth ring and retired on a high note.

Throughout his career as a head coach, he has maintained a relationship with Gregg Popovich, even joining Pop as an assistant during the 2019 FIBA World Cup and the 2020 Tokyo Olympics.

His nine titles (five as a player and four as a coach) ties him with Pat Riley for sixth all time.

#2742. Yankees reportedly re-sign lefty reliever Tim Hill to 1-year, $2.25 million deal

The New York Yankees began their offseason ambition to keep their bullpen together by re-signing reliever Tim Hill to a one-year, $2.25 million contract, according to reports.

The deal also reportedly includes a $2.5 million club option for 2028. However, the Yankees have not yet officially announced the signing.

Hill, who will turn 37 during spring training next year, compiled a 4.60 ERA with 25 strikeouts and 54 hits (12 of them home runs) allowed in 58 2/3 innings. While those numbers might not pop out as impressive, the sidearming left-hander held left-handed batters to a .213 batting average and .611 OPS.

The Yankees left Hill off their wild-card and division series rosters because of his performance.

However, the team believes that the left-hander can improve toward his 2025 numbers, when he compiled a 3.09 ERA and allowed a .181/.224/.220 slash average versus left-handed batters. Based on his overall metrics, the Yankees feel that Hill's issue with giving up home runs was the outlier, according to The Athletic.

In his nine MLB seasons, Hill has a 3.94 ERA while averaging 68 appearances. He has also pitched for the Kansas City Royals (who drafted him in the 32nd round in 2014), San Diego Padres and Chicago White Sox during his career.

Yankees relievers had the second-best ERA in MLB at 3.13 and ranked fifth with a .213 opponents' batting average. Yet Hill, Paul Blackburn Ryan Yarbrough, John Schreiber, Michael Fulmer and closer David Bednar all became free agents after the season ended.

Keeping the bullpen together — or improving on it — is "our main priority," general manager Brian Cashman said on MLB Network Radio during lat week's GM Meetings. The Yankees are also pursuing relievers Adrián Morejón (2.83 ERA, 11.2 strikeouts per 9 innings last season) and Jonathan Loáisiga (2.42 ERA), MLB.com reports.

#2741. Kyrie Irving reflects on 2016 championship in return to Cleveland: ‘I’m so grateful for all the Cavs fans’

CLEVELAND — Robert Frost said, "Home is the place where, when you have to go there, they have to take you in."

There was a time when Cleveland Cavaliers fans weren't eager to take Kyrie Irving in. His trade demands in the summer of 2017 derailed the franchise's best era of basketball. But time heals all wounds. Irving will always have a home in Cleveland — that's what delivering a championship ensures you.

"You know, 52 years is a long, long period of time waiting for a championship, and we did it," Irving said after Dallas Mavericks 127-117 loss to Cleveland on Saturday. "And I think that's one of the hardest championships — I think. I'm not gonna say it is, I said I think personally it's one of the hardest championships ever in NBA history that we got to accomplish."

It's also a championship the city is still celebrating. On Saturday, the Cavs recognized the 10th anniversary of the 2016 championship by bringing out former general manager David Griffin to pull out the sword at the start of the game. They also played a video tribute commemorating Irving's championship-clinching shot in Game 7, which brought the crowd to its feet.

"This one felt a lot different than the other times," Irving said of the reception. "This time I got to really settle in, really ground myself, and embrace the support that's here for me."

CLEVELAND, OH – APRIL 25: Byron Scott, Head Coach of the Cleveland Cavaliers talks with Kyrie Irving #2 during a break in the action against the Washington Wizards at The Quicken Loans Arena on April 25, 2012 in Cleveland, Ohio. NOTE TO USER: User expressly acknowledges and agrees that, by downloading and/or using this Photograph, user is consenting to the terms and conditions of the Getty Images License Agreement. Mandatory Copyright Notice: Copyright 2012 NBAE (Photo by David Liam Kyle/NBAE via Getty Images) | NBAE via Getty Images

Irving's time in Cleveland was far from straightforward — he detailed how when asked about it on Saturday.

His rookie season came during the lockout that lasted until December. This meant that he couldn't communicate with anyone on the coaching staff or front office. "So my first entry into here was a little awkward."

Things didn't get easier after that. The criticism he received when the Cavs initially fell short of expectations is something he still carries.

"When they're discussing my career," Irving said, "everybody likes to gear towards my first three years in the league, and like to put, 'He's not a winner. He didn't win anything. He didn't do anything without Bron (LeBron James). He didn't do anything without the players we ended up winning a championship with,' which I think is all a good and fair criticism, but I think as I look at it, it was just me learning."

The idea that a 21-year-old guard could single-handedly turn around a team that frankly wasn't very talented was unreasonable at the time, and looks even more absurd in retrospect.

James's return in 2014 didn't lighten the burden. The expectations, externally and internally, only grew. Irving admitted that this led to tough conversations with his teammates. "You don't go home happy all the time even when you're winning, because you feel like you can still continue to get better."

"What I remember most is that championship was so tough."

The championship season was a difficult one for Irving. He fractured his kneecap during Game 1 of the 2015 Finals, which kept him out until December of the following season. And when he did come back, he thought he wasn't playing to his standards. "It felt like there was a lot of pressure on me to perform well."

This was a theme for him and that team throughout a turbulent 2016 season that saw them switch coaches from David Blatt to Ty Lue halfway through.

"It's all on me at times, or it's all on Bron, or it's all on somebody else, K Love," Irving said. "It's like the blame game got split between us three, but it usually came between Bron and me. It just prepared me. You gotta be an MFer in this league. You gotta have short-term memory."

Those difficult times paid off in the championship. The 3-1 comeback was made possible by three of the best individual performances by him and James — culminating in Irving's shot to decide Game 7.

"When I look at it now, I'm just grateful that I was playing against the best of the best, going against the best teams in the world, getting everybody's best shot for about three straight years," Irving said. "A lot of young players never get to experience that, being on the hunt for championships every single year. I take those little moments and try my best to connect it to the fans here, because we couldn't have done it without them."

It's been a decade since the championship and nine years since Irving last wore a Cavs jersey, yet, the bond between the player and city may be stronger now than ever. Saturday's reception, and what it meant to Irving, is proof of that.

"It's been 10 years. It's been a great reflection point for me to be able to come back and be able to receive that support in a different way," Irving said. "I'm so grateful for all the Cavs fans, Cleveland in general, Ohio, wrapping their arms around me as well, and also sticking it through with me as I was a young person."

There were only a handful of people waiting for Irving after he filed out of the Mavs media interview room an hour and a half after the game ended. One of them politely thanked Irving for 2016 and all that it meant to them.

Irving graciously looked down at them with a wide smile and said, "We're champions forever."

#2738. Houston Texans vs. Cleveland Browns recap: A big comeback at home leads to Browns win

It was all Texans to begin this game, as their offense couldn't be stopped and Cleveland was down 17-0 early. But then, the switch flipped, and the Browns scored 20 unanswered to lead 20-17. A little back-and-forth saw the Browns re-take the lead in the final minute, 27-24, with the Texans needing a field goal to tie. The defense held on, and Cleveland won to improve to 6-4! That also includes being 4-0 at home, with the bye coming up and then three more homes games in a row.

The Cleveland Browns got the opening kickoff, with QB Deshaun Watson at the helm. After a quick 7-yard pass to TE Harold Fannin, a swing pass to RB Quinshon Judkins was dropped for a loss of 2 yards, and then Watson's third-down pass sailed over WR KC Concepcion, leading to a punt.

Cleveland allowed a 22-yard return on their punt, allowing the Texans to start at the 39 yard line. Their offense went fast then: an 8-yard run by RB Woody Marks, a 29-yard pass to WR Xavier Hutchinson, and a 13-yard pass to WR Nico Collins to set up 1st-and-goal at the 8 yard line. An illegal formation penalty, followed by a run, put the Texans in 2nd-and-goal at the 12 yard line. It didn't matter: QB C.J. Stroud tossed a perfect pass over the middle to Collins, just beyond the reach of CB Tyson Campbell, for the 12-yard touchdown, as the Browns gave up their league-leading sixth opening drive touchdown this season to put Houston up 7-0.

The Browns also went three-and-out on their next drive, as WR Jerry Jeudy couldn't haul in an intermediate pass on 2nd-and-7. Then, on third down, Concepcion caught a pass short of the marker, and the defender made a good tackle to prevent a first down. P Corey Bojorquez angled his second punt to the boundary, going out of bounds at the 12 yard line.

Stroud hit Collins on a crossing pattern against CB Myles Harden for an immediate 22-yard gain. A few plays later, on 3rd-and-1, a simple playfake and quick toss to Marks gained 15 yards. Even on a broken play after that, Stroud just took off, wide open, over the middle for 7 yards. Two plays later, on 3rd-and-3, Stroud's sideline pass for WR Tank Dell was incomplete, leading to a field goal try by K Ka'imi Fairbairn. He hit from 45 yards out to put the Texans up 10-0 with 4:25 to go in the first quarter.

On the Browns' third drive, an 8-yard run by Judkins was followed by a 1-yard run by RB Dylan Sampson, and then a quarterback sneak by Watson picked up the team's first first down of the game. A few plays later, Watson threw it behind Concepcion, leading to the defender knocking the ball away. On third down, Watson had to scramble, but only got 8.5 yards, leading to another punt. This time, Bojorquez' angled punt was only 30 yards, going out of bounds at the 25 yard line.

In one play, the Texans got right back to where Cleveland just punted from. Stroud faced no pressure, then calmly rolled to the sideline, waited, and was able to hit Dell for 29 yards. Then, to close out the quarter, he again took off for 11 more yards to the Browns' 35 yard line, with Cleveland being out-gained 159 to 34 yards.

The rather pathetic performance by the defense continued to start the second quarter, including a bizarre play when DT Maliek Collins had Stroud in his arms for a sack, then let him go to allow Stroud to throw the ball away. Several plays later, on a playaction rollout, Stroud threw the ball back to the inside for Hutchinson, who was wide open for an 8-yard touchdown to put the Texans up 17-0.

On the Browns' fourth drive, Watson launched one deep to Concepcion, and he got both hands on it as he dove to the ground, but then lost it for an incompletion. After that, Watson hit Fannin for 17 yards, and then Watson scrambled for 20 yards on the next play. The drive went no further after that, but K Andre Szmyt gave the Browns their first points of the day with a 49-yard field goal to make it 17-3.

The Browns had CB Denzel Ward on Collins to start the next drive. Stroud targeted Collins all three plays, and it led to a pass breakup, a 5-yard gain, and then a tightly-contested 6-yard gain to move the chains. After that, the Browns finally forced a punt, in part thanks to LB Carson Schwesinger bringing down Dell for a loss of 4 yards on second down. WR Denzel Boston, in for the dinged up Concepcion, fielded the punt at the 13 yard line and returned it 16 yards to the Browns' 29 yard line with 5:05 remaining in the half.

For the second drive in a row, Watson launched another bomb that was beautifully placed for Jeudy, and he hauled it in for a 65-yard gain.

One play later, Watson faked it to Judkins and then kept it himself, going around the right edge, for a 6-yard touchdown run to make it a 17-10 game with 4:12 left in the half.

On the Texans return, they were flagged 15 yards for a personal foul after the kickoff, pushing them back to the 19 yard line. Marks began with an 11-yard run to give them some more comfortable position. A 12-yard connection to Collins eventually set up 2nd-and-10 heading into the two-minute warning.

The Texans kept marching on, but then Stroud tried to hit Collins on a slant that he got jammed up in, allowing CB Tyson Campbell to come up with an interception with 39 seconds to go at their own 21 yard line.

With all three timeouts, the Browns got to their own 38 yard line on two plays, burning two timeouts, with 22 seconds left. A 16-yard strike to Boston, and the use of their final timeout, got the ball to the Texans' 46 yard line with 16 seconds left. Cleveland needed a completion and spike to have a field goal chance. Stunningly, the Texans had a weird defense, and Watson overthrew an open Jeudy that could have gone for a touchdown.

Now, with 11 seconds left, the Texans were guarding the sideline. Cleveland ran a quarterback draw for some reason to take the game into the half.

The Browns' defensive pressure was present to start the second half, forcing Stroud into three incompletions and a quick punt. An illegal block in the back penalty on Boston's punt return forced the Browns to begin at the 21 yard line. Unfortunately, they also went three-and-out after Watson was sacked for the first time on the day on third down. After the punt, and a hold on the Texans, Houston took over at their own 22 yard line.

Houston began their next drive with two runs, setting up 3rd-and-5. After a false start by Houston, the Browns' defense finally sacked Stroud as DE Isaiah McGuire got to him for a loss of 9 yards. Boston returned the punt 9 yards to the Texans' 47 yard line.

Watson hit a returning Concepcion for 8 yards to set up 3rd-and-1. Cleveland ran a playfake, and hit WR Malachi Corley for his first reception of the season for 18 yards. Then, they ran a similar play the other way to Fannin for 8 yards, plus a facemask penalty tacked on to set up 1st-and-goal at the 6 yard line. Cleveland ran a fake pitch, then handoff to Concepcion going the other way, for a 6-yard touchdown run to tie the game at 17-17 with 8:35 remaining in the third quarter.

The Browns' defensive pressure continued on the next drive, setting up a 3rd-and-12. Stroud got outside the pocket after more pressure, but was just run out of bounds to force another punt, an incredible change from the first half. Boston fair caught the punt at the 18 yard line, something he regretted because he had room for a normal return.

Cleveland ran the same play to Corley for another 8 yards, then a quick pass to Jeudy for 9 yards to move the chains. After that, Concepcion couldn't pull in a contested catch. After an 8-yard run by Judkins, Cleveland hurried up, and Watson rolled to his right and found Corley on an improvised play for 11 yards to past midfield. After a drop by Fannin, a 5-yard pass to WR Tylan Wallace set up 3rd-and-5. Watson found Sampson on the wheel route for 18 yards and a first down to the Texans' 23 yard line.

A 5 yard run by Watson, and 3-yard run by Sampson, made it 3rd-and-2. Judkins got another first down with a run on the left side, bringing up 1st-and-goal at the 10 yard line. A 2-yard run was followed by a tipped pass, setting up 3rd-and-goal at the 8 yard line. Watson faced a blitz up the middle right away, so he threw the ball away. Szmyt came on for a 26-yard field goal, and he connected to give the Browns their first lead of the day at 20-17 with 0:12 left in the third quarter.

Houston began at the 27 yard line. They ran the ball to end the quarter, and incredible, the Browns were now out-gaining the Texans 288 to 254 yards.

Starting with a 2nd-and-7, LB Quincy Williams missed a pick six over the middle, as the ball hit him right between the numbers. On third down, the quick slant to Collins was knocked away by Campbell, forcing a fourth straight punt (and three-and-out).

Cleveland took over at their 34 yard line. After a Watson keeper for 6 yards, on 3rd-and-4, he hit Judkins on the swing pass for 21 yards to the Texans' 39 yard line. A screen pass to Sampson then got 5 more yards, and then a run by him went for no gain. On 3rd-and-5, Watson tried scrambling, but was sacked on the pursuit to the edge for a loss of 2 yards. Then, it was decision time at the 37 yard line. Todd Monken sent Szmyt out for a 54-yard field goal try, and it was wide to the left, giving the Texans good field position at their own 44 yard line.

A false start by Houston made them start with a 1st-and-15. Stroud was incomplete for Collins, then faced an unblocked Schwesinger on a blitz and had to throw it away. On 3rd-and-15, Stroud lobbed a deep ball for Hutchinson that was picked by S Emmanuel McNeil-Warren, but unfortunately, while Harden was locked up with Hutchinson, the officials flagged Harden for defensive pass interference on a questionable call. It was a 37-yard penalty, and that was Houston's first first down of the half.

They got another one on the ensuing play with a 13-yard run to the Browns' 11 yard line. Two plays later, Stroud hit TE Dalton Schultz up the seam for a touchdown to put the Texans back on top 23-20. An unsportsmanlike penalty was called on the touchdown celebration, enforced on the extra point from 48 yards. But Fairbairn still hit it to make it 24-20 with 9:17 left in the game.

On the Browns' first play, Watson was sacked by DE Will Anderson for a loss of 9 yards. A quick out to Fannin got 8 yards back, making it 3rd-and-11. A 14-yard strike to Fannin got the first down. A few plays later, facing 3rd-and-3 at the Texans' 48 yard line, Watson got Anderson to jump with the hard count for a first down.

Cleveland was starting to eat up clock, now with 6:08 to go, so they needed a touchdown on this drive. A late pass to the wideline for Judkins gained 15 yards. Off of playaction, Corley was an option but covered, so Watson threw back inside to TE Blake Whiteheart, but it was beyond and off his hands, popped in the air, and intercepted at the 26 yard line with 5:12 to go.

Now the defense needed another key stop. They got two stops to force a 3rd-and-11, with clock running. Stroud tried to get it to Collins on the rollout, but it was behind him and he couldn't hang on. Houston's punt only went 26 yards, out of bounds at the Browns' 49 yard line with 3:35 to go.

Watson tried dumping it to Sampson to begin the drive, but it was short and incomplete. On second down, a quick pass to Wallace picked up 4 yards, making it 3rd-and-6 in what is four-down territory potentially. Watson was sacked for a loss of 11 yards, taking the fourth down option out of the equation. The Browns rushed the punt unit on to punt before the two-minute warning and 2:08 to go, and the ball at the 15 yard line.

Not to beat a dead horse, but this one was do-or-die for the defense in needing a stop. They ran for no gain heading into the two-minute warning, with it being 2nd-and-10. Houston ran another run play for no gain, as the Browns burned their first timeout. On 3rd-and-10, Stroud took hit shot for Schultz, but it was overthrown to force a punt and Cleveland keeping two timeouts in their pocket.

After the punt, the Browns got the ball at the Texans' 49 yard line with 1:40 to go. Watson had all day to throw on first down, and hit Sampson for a loss of 1 yard. Watson then tried hitting Wallace to the corner, but there was a flag for a hit on a defenseless receiver, a 15 yard penalty and a first down at the 35 yard line with 1:11 to go. Watson then drilled Fannin for a 30-yard connection to the 5-yard line, as now Houston took their first timeout with 0:56 to go.

One play later, Judkins punched it in to put the Browns back on top 26-24.

The all-important extra point by Szymt was good to make it 27-24 with 0:53 left and Houston having two timeouts, needing a field goal. They began at the 15 yard line. Stroud threw the ball away on first down after immediate pressure. On second down, Stroud had to dodge pressure, and right before going out of bounds, found a target for 13 yards with 0:34 left.

Browns defenders made two straight great defensive pass breakups, forcing a third down. Stroud overthrew Collins on the sideline, making it fourth down with 0:17 left. Stroud dodged pressure and got a completion to Collins for 16 yards, but a hold brought it back for 4th-and-20 with 10 seconds left. Stroud launched one for Hutchinton, and it was incomplete with one second to go. Watson came back on to take a knee.

QB Deshaun Watson: 25-of-40 for 312 yards, 1 INT. 8 rushes, 49 yards, 1 TD.

WR KC Concepcion: 3 catches, 21 yards. 1 carry, 6 yards, 1 TD.

This is a huge win for the Browns. Big credit to them for adjusting and rallying, and dominating the last three quarters of this game. It was another one-score win, but I started to see the formula I was looking for if this team is going to turn the corner for more dominant wins in the future.

Up next, the Browns have their bye week, and then host the Las Vegas Raiders in Week 12.

#2732. 3 instant takeaways from the Chiefs’ beatdown of the Falcons

In Week 10, the Kansas City Chiefs defeated the Atlanta Falcons in dominant fashion. The 35-13 finish put the Chiefs at 7-2. Here is the rapid reaction to the game:

If it wasn't clear before Sunday that the Chiefs have one of the NFL's best backfield duos, rookie running back Emmett Johnson solidified that narrative with 163 yards from scrimmage and two touchdowns over 25 touches. He runs hard off the snap to break through arm tackles and always fights forward; on top of that, he remains a reliable pass-catcher.

The reassurance that Kansas City can keep the defense threatened by the backfield when Kenneth Walker is huge for the offense. The Chiefs' offense has been more disciplined with the run game and going under center this season, but the talent boost from last season has made the biggest difference. Johnson would have been one of Kansas City's best offensive players last year.

In his first game in a Chiefs uniform, wide receiver Josh Downs made an impact without needing many snaps. He saw five passes thrown his way, turning them into 53 yards and a late-half touchdown that put Kansas City up 14-3.

The score featured Mahomes stepping up in the pocket and rifling it to Downs with a defender near, targeting his back shoulder with confidence. It's the kind of throw Kansas City's wide receivers haven't warranted from Mahomes, but once he made it, he couldn't stop pushing the ball downfield.

Mahomes finished the game with seven passes that gained 20 or more yards, and it seemed like a different player caught each one. In the second half, rookie wide receiver Cyrus Allen shook cornerback A.J. Terrell on the outside and broke wide open down the left sideline for a touchdown. On the next possession, Mahomes pushed it deep down the middle to Rashee Rice on a post route; the veteran made a strong catch, one of the most impressive of his career.

Those confident throws happened in the timing of the play, but then he uncorked a 43-yard touchdown bomb to Andrew Armstrong as he drew out a scramble drill. When it comes to throwing downfield — something we haven't seen from Mahomes in over a month — it seems he needs confidence in his receivers to make these plays happen, and Downs may have been the catalyst for that confidence in Atlanta.

From start to finish, outside of one drive early in the fourth quarter, the Chiefs' defense played lights out in Atlanta. The Falcons never sustained a drive for 10 or more plays and went three-and-out on five of the first six possessions. In the first half, Atlanta had just four first downs and converted only one of seven third-down attempts.

The pass rush recorded three sacks, bringing the total to eight over the last two blowout wins. Defensive end George Karlaftis has been on a tear, adding to his team-leading sacks (5.5) and passes-defended marks (10), per Pro Football Reference.

The Chiefs' cornerbacks were also in shutdown mode; Nohl Williams and rookie Mansoor Delane combined for four passes broken up, and Williams nabbed an interception at the end of the game.

#2730. Panthers pull out 20-17 win in New Orleans, take lead in NFC South

NFC South football has earned a negative reputation over the last few years and Sunday’s game between the Panthers and Saints didn’t do much to change that, but it did push one of the teams into first place in the race to represent the division in the playoffs.

After the Saints rallied to tie the game on a Charlie Smyth field goal with 41 seconds left to play, the Panthers took over on their own 34-yard-line. Bryce Young threw an incompletion and then uncorked a deep shot to wide receiver Jalen Coker. Saints corner Kool-Aid McKinstry was flagged for pass interference and that set up a 54-yard field goal as time expired.

Ryan Fitzgerald had missed from 48 yards in the first half, but he nailed this kick and the Panthers are in first place after a 20-17 win.

There’s some irony in the fact that a penalty helped put the Panthers in position to win the game. Carolina committed 17 penalties during the contest, including a facemask call on linebacker Tyrel Dodson that helped set up Smyth’s game-tying kick.

The Saints did their best to match that sloppiness with a pair of turnovers in the first half and they focused on the running game well beyond the point when it became clear that they weren’t going to generate the kind of production needed on that front. Travis Etienne, Audric Estime, and Alvin Kamara combined to pick up 24 yards on 17 carries on Sunday.

Their offense picked up a bit once they leaned on throwing the ball and rookie wideout Jordyn Tyson wound up with 11 catches in his second game of the year, but the overall approach was still lacking against a Panthers team that has not been very stout defensively this season.

New Orleans will be in Chicago next Sunday and they’ll need a win there to try to keep themselves in stalking distance of the Panthers heading into the stretch run.

Carolina improved to 4-5 with the win and they will host the Ravens in Week 11. They’d be wise to continue leaning on Chuba Hubbard in that contest. Hubbard had 27 carries for 92 yards and two touchdowns along with five catches for 80 yards. That accounted for 172 of the Panthers’ 283 total yards, so Carolina’s best chance of winning games appears to be relying on their workhorse running back.

#2728. Buffalo Bills stock report in big victory over New York Jets

The Buffalo Bills and the New York Jets played a hard-fought game this afternoon. Truthfully, it was typical of games between these two clubs, as the feisty Jets played tough against a heavily-favored Bills team. Any time a divisional game is happening, you can throw out the Vegas line, and that was exactly what happened today.

The Jets kept things close early on, as the score was 10-9 in favor of the Bills early in the third. Buffalo answered quickly, scoring a touchdown on their first possession of the second half to extend their lead to 17-9.

While the Jets fought hard, their inability to score touchdowns eventually came back to bite them, as Buffalo continued to expand their lead as the game progressed. Ultimately, Buffalo prevailed, winning 31-12 in a game that featured quite a few injuries for both sides.

Here are our horns up and horns down for Buffalo in today's game against the Jets._____________________________________________________________________________

Throughout this season, false start penalties have been a problem. Early on today, it was jumping offside that proved to be an issue. After the Jets' first possession, Michael Hoecht jumped offside while the Jets lined up to punt. While it did not give the Jets a first down, it gave them five extra yards to set up a more comfortable punt. Later, on a fourth-down on the Jets' second drive, Ed Oliver jumped on a fourth-and-six where it was fairly clear that the Jets weren't going to run a play. That gave New York a fourth-and-one, which they converted en route to what became a field goal drive. Pre-snap penalties are a matter of discipline, and too often this year, the Bills have not passed the test.

Well, all we had to do was wait for the second quarter for the false start penalties to kick in. Dawson Knox and Dion Dawkins were each called for one on what became a field goal drive. Four pre-snap penalties in the first half alone is maddening. Alec Anderson committed a fifth one with a false start in the fourth quarter. Heck, Hoecht tried to read the snap count and jumped offside for a second time in the game while the Jets were driving in the fourth quarter.

Another week, another game where Buffalo's top running back looked great. Cook found holes, was decisive throughout, and gained plenty of yardage on the ground. If there was a negative, it was that he dropped what would have at least been an easy completion, and potentially could have been a first down or more, while Buffalo was in the red zone during the second quarter. Cook is a dynamic receiving threat, but he just has too many concentration drops to be considered a reliable threat in that area. Cook's 74-yard touchdown run in the fourth quarter put the game away, as well. Overall he had exactly the kind of game that we've come to expect from him, totaling 157 rushing yards and the aforementioned touchdown on 18 carries.

The former Tulane Green Wave standout had another solid day. He was the one who notched Buffalo's first sack of Geno Smith, timing a blitz perfectly to take down the Jets' signal caller in the second quarter. Williams has been playing quite well over the last month or so, and he led the Bills in tackles through the first half of play this week. He also was able to put a hit on Cade Klubnik to force an incomplete pass that Amani Hooker nearly intercepted on New York's first possession of the second half. Williams left the game in the fourth quarter with a calf injury, and here's hoping that it was mostly precautionary that he didn't return. He finished with a team-high nine tackles, including five solo stops, one sack, one pass breakup, and two quarterback hits.

Tyler Bass nailed a PAT and yet another field goal in the first half. That field goal extended his franchise-record streak of made field goals, as it was the 24th straight kick he's made. Tommy Doman Jr. hit some great kickoffs and good punts, as he seems to have mastered the concept of "hangtime" on his punts. Focusing on the punter is ever a good thing, but in an ugly first half, Doman was a bright spot. He pinned another one deep in Jets territory in the fourth quarter, bouncing a high boot inside the ten-yard line. Bass finished with just that one field goal and four made PATs, while Doman landed all three of his punts inside the Jets' 20-yard line, averaging 46.3 yards per punt in the process.

For much of the early going, there was little pressure on either Jets quarterback. Geno Smith had time to throw, completing 9 of the 16 passes he attempted in the first half before he was forced out of the game with an upper-body injury. When rookie Cade Klubnik entered the game, the Bills still couldn't create pressure, and the mobile 23-year old was able to make them pay on the Jets' first possession out of the half. The pass rush plan can't be "let's hope Greg Rousseau makes a play." Sometimes, defensive coordinator Jim Leonhard needs to dial up some different looks, especially when the front four or five players aren't creating enough pressure.

In the second half, Leonhard started to dial it up a notch, and whether that was due to Smith's injury or not is irrelevant. Buffalo was able to create some pressure through blitzes when the rookie Klubnik was in the game. After a slow start, Buffalo finished with four sacks and 12 QB hits on the day.

While the Bills allowed the Jets to drive quite a bit, they were very efficient when it came to keeping the Jets out of the end zone. Part of that could be because the Jets are among the NFL's worst red zone offenses, but it doesn't change the fact that Buffalo kept holding the Jets to three points instead of six when Gang Green was close to scoring touchdowns. The Jets were just 3-for-13 on third downs, and Buffalo held their opponents out of the end zone once again. The Bills haven't allowed a touchdown in 11 quarters, dating back to their victory over the Baltimore Ravens.

Buffalo's top wideout made a couple of big plays in this one. In the first half, Moore caught a pass on a crossing route to convert a first down, traveling 34 yards to set up the Bills' first touchdown. Then, on the Bills' second possession of the second half, Moore caught a 45-yard touchdown pass on a beautifully thrown ball from Josh Allen. On the former, Moore was slammed hard to the turf, and for a moment, I was worried that he injured his left shoulder after dealing with an injury to his right shoulder for most of this season. On the latter, Moore lost the football while crossing the goal line, and it wasn't immediately clear that the pass was caught. However, both plays worked out for the Bills. Moore finished his day with four catches for 90 yards and a touchdown on six targets. All of those numbers led the Bills today.

The rookie continued his streak of solid play, as he was strong in coverage regardless of which Jets wideout he was covering. Igbinosun also notched his second career interceptions, picking off Klubnik in the third quarter. Unfortunately for Igbinosun, he was absolutely smoked on the return by Jets tight end Jeremy Ruckert. Igbinosun fumbled, and the Jets recovered it, negating the big play and giving the Jets the ball back. New York eventually punted, but Buffalo forfeited plenty of field position. He finished with four tackles and two pass breakups. He was once again excellent in coverage.

MetLife Stadium is a house of horrors for many, as its turf is notorious for causing injury. By the end of today's game, Buffalo was without Dorian William (calf), Christian Benford (groin), Dee Alford (concussion), Cole Bishop (arm), and Maxwell Hairston (unknown at time of publication). Let's hope that these players were out due to the time remaining in the game and the score at the time of said injuries. Otherwise, Buffalo will trot out something akin to a M.A.S.H. unit next week against the Miami Dolphins.

The rookie had the best day of his young career, totaling two sacks, five tackles, and three quarterback hits. After initially leaving me whining about the pass rush early in this game, Buffalo settled into the game and really got after rookie quarterback Kade Klubnik. For the Bills, it was nice to see Parker step up and make some plays. If he can start to serve as a valuable counter to Greg Rousseau on the other side of the line, it bodes well for Buffalo's pass rush and defense as a whole moving forward.

Alright, friends…who did we miss? Our Bills are 8-1 after today's big win. Let us know who deserves a shout-out in either direction in the comments!

#2726. Milwaukee Bucks vs. Atlanta Hawks Preview & Game Thread: A chance at linear progress

The Milwaukee Bucks try to repeat their competitive form tonight back at home against the Atlanta Hawks. Look, the Bucks are obviously not playing well and are on a nine-game losing streak, but this season will be about taking the small wins and worrying about process over results. Their last game against the Magic was a clear improvement over the previous four, and fans should hope they can repeat that effort tonight. I'm looking for effort, intensity, and stick-to-itiveness.

The Hawks have had an underwhelming start to the year, sitting at 7-6. In terms of their recent form, they oh-so-nearly lost to the Nets (yes, the Nets), then dropped their next games to the Lakers and, surprisingly, the Bulls. So why have the Hawks underwhelmed? A key reason has been Nickeil Alexander-Walker, who has yet to rediscover last season's Most Improved Player-winning form. After trading Trae Young in the middle of last year, the Hawks turned to Alexander-Walker to replace his production, which he did and then some. Unfortunately for Hawks fans, NAW has had a rough start to the 2026-27 campaign, shooting his worst field-goal percentage in three seasons at 40.8%. Moreover, his three-point shooting has dropped to 35.0%, down from the near-40% mark he had been at. Additionally, Wisconsin native and one-time All-Star Jalen Johnson has also struggled, shooting just 19.0% (!) from deep. The Hawks will be hoping to use tonight's contest as a get-right game for these two in particular, and the Bucks can't let that happen.

Brayden Burries quickly moved into the starting lineup for the Bucks and immediately made an impact, averaging 13.0 PPG, 8.3 RPG, and 3.5 APG across his first four starts. In those contests, he averaged 11.8 shot attempts per game. However, in the three games since, those numbers have dropped to 4.7 PPG, 4.0 RPG, and 2.3 APG on just 5.7 shot attempts per game. While it's good that Burries isn't forcing questionable shots like some of his veteran teammates, it does feel he's being a bit too passive at times. Part of this comes down to role—Burries is being played out of position at the point (which will likely have long-term benefits)—but part of it is on him, perhaps placing too much emphasis on running the action and prioritising ball security at the cost of aggressiveness. Tonight, then, pay particular attention to how assertive he is. This doesn't necessarily mean shooting. But how often is he touching paint to collapse the defence? And, given how Burries has said that "rebounding gets [him] going," how hard is he crashing the glass?

Who: Milwaukee Bucks (1-11) vs. Atlanta Hawks (7-6).

2024-25 NBA steals leader and current NBA steals leader Dyson Daniels has a field day in the passing lanes, taking advantage of the Bucks' turnover issues on his way to a season-high-tying seven steals.

#2725. Belgium come from behind to beat Italy in Nations League

Belgium came from behind to earn a comprehensive 4-1 win over Italy and progress through to the quarter-finals of the Nations League.

Mark van Bommel's side displayed their attacking qualities and comfortably swept Italy aside as they were dumped out of the competition after finishing third.

Francesco Pio Esposito opened the scoring for Italy but Belgium responded in brilliant fashion and quickly got their equalizer through Maxim De Cuyper.

Belgium sensed blood and hit the front courtesy of Nathan Ngoy's first international goal before Jeremy Doku's brilliant individual finish gave them a two-goal lead at the break.

Belgium were keen not to let their foot off the throat of the Italians and they got their fourth through Diego Moreira, who also scored his first national team goal before Pio Esposito saw a late penalty saved.

Italy forced the first effort on target in the seventh minute when Niccolo Pisilli latched on to Riccardo Calafiori's cross but was unable to guide it past Senne Lammens.

And they hit the front four minutes later after Pio Esposito got on the end of Luca Koleosho's cross and this time there was nothing Lammens could do to stop Italy from scoring.

They were only behind for a matter of minutes before the home side equalized, a slick move finished at the feet of De Cuyper, who tucked home from close range.

Belgium turned the game on its head in the 21st minute as Doku's initial strike was parried into the danger zone by Gianluigi Donnarumma and Ngoy reacted quickest to get their second of the night.

Belgium had a two-goal cushion five minutes later as Doku received a pass from Kevin De Bruyne and showed neat footwork to fashion himself the opportunity before dispatching in the bottom left corner.

The Red Devils could have had a fourth but De Bruyne was denied by Donnarumma just six yards out.

The hosts continued to look for more goals after the break as well. Youri Tielemans set up De Bruyne who fired wide with his second big opportunity of the game.

The crossbar came to Italy's rescue shortly after when Charles De Ketelaere jumped highest to a cross but nodded off the upright before De Bruyne was once again frustrated by Donnarumma.

Italy had been let off the hook on a number of occasions but Belgium finally got their fourth with 10 minutes to go.

The Italians committed men up the field and Belgium broke through Doku and he sprayed a pass into De Bruyne who rolled it to Moreira, and he expertly tucked beyond Donnarumma to settle the contest.

Italy's night got worse after they were awarded a late penalty, with Pio Esposito unable to convert from 12 yards.
