#2721. NBA: Anyone Hungry for Taco Bell?

"What If" Wolves Volume 7: Draft bust? What's that?

Very few franchises have employed more draft busts than our beloved Minnesota Timberwolves. The poor drafting started right away, and unfortunately has continued all the way up to the drafting of Rob Dillingham in 2024. But our Wolves don't just draft busts, we also trade for them and give them huge contracts in free agency.

The list of unfulfilled potential is long. Here's a quick list to refresh your mind of the hot garbage that has graced the hardwood at Target Center:

And those are just some of the first round draft picks. We couldn't stop with just draft picks though. We just had to trade for and try to fix everyone else's garbage.

Darko "Manna from heaven" Miličić (2003 – 2nd pick)

Cody Williams (2024 – 10th pick) (too soon?)

Now I know some of you may be thinking Luc Longley wasn't that bad…for the Chicago Bulls. McCants and Foye were decent players (they weren't.) Ndubi Ebi? Didn't he have six fingers? Point is: we were terrible at drafting players, trading for players, and signing players. But there is one draft pick worse than all of these.

Derrick Williams was the number two overall pick for the Wolves in the 2011 NBA Draft. Now this is rare for me, but I am going to be completely honest here: I was all about drafting Derrick Williams. Williams was the consensus second pick, right behind Duke's Kyrie Irving. Hell, some people even had Williams as the number one pick.

Williams was an All-American power forward from the University of Arizona. He led the Wildcats to the Elite Eight his sophomore year, even upsetting Kyrie and the Blue Devils with a stat line of 32 points and 13 rebounds. He was a "can't miss" prospect with a solid inside and outside game, explosive to the basket, and was a great rebounder for his size at 6'8". Over a two year college career, Derrick made 42 of 74 three-point attempts. For those of you that struggle with math, that's 56.8%.

The 2011-12 Timberwolves roster was kind of a mess. Kevin Love was the best player on the team with averages of 26 points and 13 rebounds per game which led to his first All-Star appearance. Ricky Rubio finally came over from Spain for his rookie year. Michael Beasley was at the small forward position, trying to revive his career. Nikola "Serbian Godfather" Peković ate all the meat. Luke Ridnour was there, nobody really noticed. And then there was Derrick Williams, a power forward in a small forward's body (or Caged Lion's body.) Too slow to take anyone to the rack (shoutout "mud runs"). Couldn't create his own shot. And his 56.8% three-point percentage from Arizona? It was still in Tucson.

Williams did have his flashes. He had a few double-doubles. Scored 27 against the Los Angeles Clippers one night in February. Derrick was on the finishing end of a few alley-oops from Ricky Rubio. But once Rubio went down with his knee injury (I would never forgive Kobe Bryant for this…or for some other issues), Williams struggled mightily.

I refuse to believe that drafting the biggest bust in franchise history can't be fixed. Afterall, this is the "What If" Wolves. We only draft winners. So let's go back in time and get rid of Derrick Williams and make this right. And I mean really right.

The 2011 NBA draft was considered at the time, a weak ass draft. It was Irving and Williams, then a bunch of other stuff. Granted, there are some Hall of Fame players that came out of this draft. Klay Thompson (11th pick), Kawhi Leonard (15th pick), previously mentioned Kyrie Irving (1st pick), and it make me sick to say this, Jimmy Butler (30th pick and hopefully doesn't get in the HOF). Because of the limited upside at the top of the draft board, I am going to take advantage of the ridiculous fandom that was surrounding BYU guard Jimmer Fredette.

Jimmer Fredette played just like Steph Curry, but the Utah beer version, aka weak. But the people in Utah couldn't get enough of Jimmer, so I make a call to the Utah Jazz and take advantage. I trade back in the draft by giving the Jazz the second overall pick for the following: 2011 tenth overall pick, 2013 unprotected first round pick, 2014 unprotected second round pick, and a 2015 unprotected second round pick. Utah moves up eight spots for what is basically an additional first and two seconds. Not a bad trade for either side. Maybe I could have gotten the Jazz to sweeten the deal, but their roster was just as bad as the Wolves at that time. With the second pick, the Jazz take Jimmer Fredette. This pick was made by the Jazz so fast that Kyrie was still shaking the David Stern's hand.

Now that I avoided the dreaded draft bust of the 2011 NBA draft, I patiently wait for the draft board to play out. With the newly acquired 10th pick, the "What If" Wolves take Klay Thompson from Washington State. I don't think that I need to breakdown Klay's game for everyone, but he was an outstanding player. Probably possessed the most perfect jumpshot in NBA history. The Wolves get Klay Thompson and we don't even have to give up Kevin Love for him. Check out this blast from the Canis past about the KLove for Klay trade here.

The 2011-12 "What If" Wolves continue to struggle, but they are actually building something. KLove and K-Lay (I know that is gross) shoot lights out from the perimeter. Rubio develops chemistry with the roster while running around on two healthy knees (Thompson would be the one guarding Kobe instead of Rubio). Let's say the Wolves finish the lockout shortened season with a 30-36 record. A solid start to the rebuild.

The next few years don't go any better for the Jazz. Jimmer would Jimmer, and the "What If" Wolves end up with their 2013 first round pick at 14th overall. The Wolves also have their own pick at number nine. With the ninth overall pick the Wolves start taking some risks. CJ McCollum will be our pick here. Despite coming out of tiny Lehigh University, CJ has made an incredible career for himself. Always a solid shooter and sometimes decent set up man.

One more risk to take with the Jazz pick from the Jimmer trade. With that pick the team looks back to team history for a bit of inspiration. The Wolves once took a chance on a skinny high school kid in 1995. After reviewing dozens of grainy VHS tapes, the Wolves take a skinny kid from Greece: Giannis Antetokounmpo. I can rewrite history however I want, so yes, I take Giannis. The real Timberwolves had the chance to draft Giannis twice. Once with the ninth overall pick (Trey Burke) which they traded to the Jazz for the 14th (Shabazz) and 21st picks (Gorgui Dieng). I right the wrong and take the Greek Freak.

With CJ and Giannis added to the "What If" Wolves, the team makes the playoffs for the first time since Kevin Garnett brought the real Timberwolves to the 2004 Western Conference Finals. The roster is starting to really look like a threat. Kevin Love, Klay Thompson, Ricky Rubio, CJ McCollum, Giannis (not spelling that last name again), Peković. Starting to look crazy scary.

Because the "What If" Wolves made the playoffs, they missed out on the 2014 lottery and the chance to redraft Zach LaVine. So let's say the Wolves end up with the 24th overall pick and take center Clint Capela. Capella has had a solid NBA career providing solid defense and averages of about 11 points and 10 rebounds per game. Not a terrible late first round pick. We also have a slew of second round picks, Utah's at 35 plus MN picks at 40, 44, and 53 overall.

Usually I would have waited for the spotlight to make this pick, but the NBA loves their sponsors. So with the 35th overall pick the "What If" Wolves take "a delicious cheesy quesadilla wrapped around a beefy burrito, the new Quesarito from Taco Bell."

That's right, during the Taco Bell commercial to push their new diarrhea inducing Quesarito, the "What If" Wolves draft Nikola Jokić!

Jokić, Giannis, Klay, CJ, and Capela to run with Ricky and Love. 82-0. All of the championships. All of them!

I think I still had some second round picks or something, but who cares? I sell all of those stupid picks for horses to give to Jokić and the rights to all of the Antetokounmpo brothers. Quesaritos on me!

#2716. Sixers (sans Embiid) begin back-to-back with matinee hosting Pacers

This is the first game of a somewhat stretched back-to-back for the Sixers, with the squad facing the Pacers early afternoon Sunday and then hitting the road to take on the Nets on Monday night.

This is the second meeting of the year between the Sixers and Pacers, with Philadelphia taking the 114-109 victory in the first matchup — helped mightily by a six-triple night from Anfernee Simons.

For this one, we already know that Joel Embiid will be out for plantar fasciitis management. LeBron James, who missed the Sixers' last contest for sciatica management, is not on the injury report and is good to go for Sunday. Adem Bona and Dominick Barlow remain sidelined. This means the possibility of seeing some more time at the five for James Sunday afternoon, one would imagine. Well, someone is going to have to stand up to Pacers' 7-foot center Ivica Zubac…

The Pacers are trying to manage things without Tyrese Haliburton, who suffered a hamstring strain just a few games into the season and has not played since. He remains out for Sunday. Ben Sheppard and Johnny Furphy are also sidelined with knee injuries. Andrew Nembhard and Aaron Nesmith are both questionable and T.J. McConnell is probable, so a lot of things up in the air for the Indiana side as it stands.

Missing Hali hasn't meant being completely unable to win, however. Despite being under .500 with a 5-8 record, the Pacers are coming into Sunday with a two-game win streak under their belt, the most recent of which being a close victory over the New York Knicks in NBA Cup action back on Friday night. The team is still working on finding their footing as they navigate this prolonged period without Haliburton, but they're finding ways to snag some wins along the way. Their victory over the Knicks was result of a widespread effort, with all five Indiana starters scoring 12 or more points.

And that's been the story with the Pacers without Haliburton. Six Indiana players (not including the sidelined star guard) are averaging double-digit scoring to start this season. Pascal Siakam has been leading the squad averaging 22.4 points per game. Nembhard leads the playmaking charge with 9.5 dimes a night in addition to his 16.6 points and 40.3% shooting from long range. Obi Toppin, Nesmith, Zubac and former Sixer Kelly Oubre Jr. round out the supporting cast all averaging around 10-12 points per night. It's not a matter of slowing down one player with the current Pacers, it's about slowing them all down.

But the bottom line is that, on paper, this is a game the Sixers should be able to win even without Embiid, especially if a few of the Pacers' currently questionable players are forced to miss this one. However, don't be surprised if it's another close one, with the Sixers needing to be on their A-game to make sure this one doesn't slip away. Even this early in the season we've already seen examples of the Sixers playing down to their competition and making games harder than they needed to be. I'm sure the Pacers would be thrilled to take advantage of that to extend their current win streak with their main star sidelined.

The Sixers and Pacers tip off their matinee contest at 1 p.m. ET.

Raiders tight end Brock Bowers left the game during the scoring drive and got checked out in the sideline medical tent before returning to action.

#2705. Clippers rookie Keaton Wagler draws praise from Shai Gilgeous-Alexander

Keaton Wagler continues to find his footing in his first season with the Los Angeles Clippers.

The fifth overall pick in the 2026 draft has found a way to contribute to coach Tyronn Lue's team, averaging 12.4 points, 3.4 rebounds and 3.2 assists through 11 games played this season.

The rookie has not made his first official start but has averaged 22.7 minutes per game off the bench.

Wagler modeled part of his game after Shai Gilgeous-Alexander, who spent his rookie season with the Clippers in 2018 before he was later traded to the Oklahoma City Thunder.

Being in the NBA has afforded Wagler opportunities early in the season to play against some of his idols with the Clippers having played the Thunder twice this season.

"It was a cool experience," Wagler told reporters about being able to play against Gilgeous-Alexander. "Growing up I really liked Shai, his game and being able to go up against him."

Wagler has drawn praise from Gilgeous-Alexander after those two meetings.

"He has good feel. Can really shoot," Gilgeous-Alexander said about the rookie after the Clippers' 113-112 victory against the Thunder on Friday, Nov. 13. "Usually when you have those things, the game will slow down for you eventually.

"He's in a good situation where he can play a lot. There are good coaches over there, so he'll learn the game the right way. As long as he works hard, he should have a long runway to be whoever he wants to be."

The Clippers have struggled to start the season, winning just three of their first 11 games, including Friday's victory against the Thunder.

Earlier this month, Lue apologized to the rookie for not getting him more playing time.

"That's just what I felt like," Lue said about Wagler's minutes after a 117-116 overtime loss to the Miami Heat on Nov. 4.

Wagler played a season-low 16 minutes against Miami. He finished with 11 points and a team-high +15 while on the court.

Lue told reporters on Nov. 8 that he had apologized to Wagler after having rewatched the film from the game, telling him he earned more playing time than he received.

Wagler averaged 19.6 minutes per game over the last three games since Lue's apology.

#2703. What We Learned From MN Vikings’ Win vs Packers; Return of Kirk Cousins

Kirk Cousins' last start for the Minnesota Vikings, prior to Sunday, ended with a torn Achilles at Lambeau Field in Week 8 of 2023. As fate would have it, Kirk's first start back in purple came on the same field this afternoon, where Cousins led the Vikings to a victory over the Green Bay Packers 30-10.

Minnesota, now 7-2, led the Packers 17-0 at halftime, before ultimately finishing with the most points its scored since the last time they played against Green Bay, a 39-22 win at home in Week 1.

The win snapped a two-game losing streak for the Vikings and leaves them in a three-way tie with the Bears and Lions atop the NFC North. Minnesota holds the division tiebreaker over Chicago, not Detroit, but they still have one game remaining against both teams.

Today, there's just one main takeaway. The Minnesota Vikings needed Kirk Cousins to fix pretty much all of their problems. After just two practices with the Vikings prior to kickoff, Kirk posted what Alec Lewis (The Athletic) called the team's best quarterback performance of the season.

FINAL // Vikings 30, Packers 10.Well, holy cow. What Kirk Cousins just did in a few days is wildly impressive. Switched systems and had the best QB'd performance of the year. Makes this team quite dangerous.The record is 7-2.

— Alec Lewis (@alec_lewis) November 15, 2026

Related: Vikings, Kirk Cousins Return Press Conference Highlights

Cousins finished 21-of-31 for 207 yards with two touchdowns and an interception, good for a 94.4 passer rating. According to ESPN Research, said he is the first quarterback since at least 1950 to start for two different teams in consecutive weeks.

Those 207 yards were only the fourth-most by a Vikings quarterback in a game this season, but well above their season average of 179.5 yards per game, coming in. Prior to Sunday, Minnesota quarterbacks were averaging 179.6 passing yards per game (8 total TD, 5 INT).

Drew Brees with high praise for @KirkCousins8 in his first game back with the Vikings. @drewbrees📺: FOX/FOX One https://t.co/0hhdxTezwx

— FOX Sports: NFL (@NFLonFOX) November 15, 2026

Where Cousins really changed the game was on third down. The Vikings came in converting just 33.3% of their third downs this season, including 3-of-18 the past two weeks. Today, with Kirko back under center, Minnesota went 8-of-16 on third down, including a couple of dimes by their familiar new QB1.

His first touchdown came on 3rd-and-23. A face-mask penalty on DeeJay Dallas pushed the Vikings back from 3rd-and-8, and Cousins hit Myles Price for 38 yards. It was Minnesota's first offensive touchdown in a first quarter this season.

JUUUUUUUUUUUUG@Myles_price3 with his 1st career receiving TD pic.twitter.com/fLoJ8NkZNL

— Minnesota Vikings (@Vikings) November 15, 2026

In the second quarter, Cousins converted a 3rd-and-15 with a 24-yard throw to Tai Felton that reached the Green Bay 1, setting up Aaron Jones' 1-yard touchdown run.

Late in the half, Xavier McKinney intercepted a deep ball for Felton at the Green Bay 16. Earlier, a Lukas Van Ness interception was overturned to a sack on replay, bailing the offense out of a really bad situation.

Over the previous two games, Minnesota's offense held the ball for an average of 20:52, and Buffalo had it for 42:32 in Week 9. On Sunday, the Vikings held it for 35:16 and ran 65 plays, up from 48 a game over that stretch.

With more time on the sideline, Brian Flores' defense posted a season-high eight sacks, two more than its previous best at Tampa Bay in Week 3.

Vikings defense doing Vikings defense things https://t.co/IHKxyxySgx

— Armando Salguero (@ArmandoSalguero) November 15, 2026

Levi Drake Rodriguez had two sacks and four quarterback hits. Eric Wilson, Elijah Williams, Andrew Van Ginkel, Dallas Turner, Kyle Van Noy and Jalen Redmond had one each, and Wilson led the team with seven tackles and two tackles for loss.

According to Ben Goessling (Star Tribune), the eight sacks are the most the Vikings have ever had at Lambeau Field, topping a record of seven set in 1983. Per Minnesota Sports Fan research, the 126 points allowed by Flores' defense this season is the least of any Vikings team in history and the least of any NFL teams since 1976.

Turner's sack was his eighth of the season, which ties his career high. By halftime, the Vikings had five sacks and two takeaways and hadn't allowed a point, and Jordan Love had been hit on 8 of 17 first-half dropbacks.

5 sacks2 takeaways0 points allowed🙌🙌🙌 pic.twitter.com/Jxdb74OMl9

— Minnesota Vikings (@Vikings) November 15, 2026

Related: Vikings Trying to Keep Kirk Cousins Beyond 2026

The defense forced four turnovers after averaging 1.6 over the first eight games. Van Ginkel forced and recovered a fumble at the Green Bay 10, Turner recovered an aborted snap at the 27, and Jakobe Thomas forced a kickoff fumble that Jake Golday recovered at the 35. All three turned into Will Reichard field goals.

The other was Harrison Smith's interception of Love in the end zone. It was the 41st of his career, moving him past Ed Sharockman into third in Vikings history behind Paul Krause (53) and Bobby Bryant (51), and his seventh against Green Bay.

Green Bay finished with 220 total yards, 56 rushing yards on 17 carries and a 3-of-10 mark on third down. Love went 17-of-27 for 220 yards with a touchdown and an interception, and the Packers' only touchdown drive needed a 3rd-and-20 and a 4th-and-10 conversion.

In other words, it was just another day for the Minnesota Vikings' defense. But this time around, the offense was there to deliver their own blow and help bury Green Bay early, and pound them deeper into the ground late.

With Cousins keeping drives alive, the Vikings ran 31 times for 129 yards (4.2 per carry). They averaged 26.4 carries for 106 yards in their first eight games, and just 15 carries for 66 yards over the last two.

Jordan Mason ran 16 times for 59 yards and closed out the game with four straight runs, including a 5-yard pickup on 4th-and-3. Aaron Jones added 47 yards on 11 carries, and after his touchdown he got the Lambeau Leap he said this week he wanted.

Aaron Jones said this week he wanted to get his Lambeau Leap in as a member of the Vikings. He found a group of Vikings fans in the front row of the north end zone and pointed to them after he scored, before Wisconsin native Max Bredeson joined him. 17-0 Vikings.

— Ben Goessling (@BenGoessling) November 15, 2026

The Vikings did leave points in the red zone. They went 1-of-4 inside the 20 after going 9-of-17 before Sunday, and two of those trips stalled on sacks, including Van Ness beating Christian Darrisaw on 3rd-and-goal in the third quarter.

Justin Jefferson caught nine passes for 74 yards on 13 targets. The catches and targets were season highs, and the yards were his second-most of the year behind the 92 he had in Week 1.

Kirk Cousins' First Throw Back As A Vikings Quarterback Went Right To Justin Jefferson.It Fell Incomplete, But You Know Exactly Who He Was Looking For.Some Things Never Change In Minnesota. https://t.co/Ajbf6FSg0U

— SM Highlights (@SMHighlights1) November 15, 2026

In his first seven games (he missed Week 4 with an ankle sprain), Jefferson averaged 4.4 catches, 56.1 yards and 7.3 targets. He declined to talk to reporters after the loss in Detroit, then caught three of 11 targets against Buffalo.

Six of his catches on Sunday came on the fourth-quarter touchdown drive, including a 16-yard screen on 3rd-and-12 after Cousins changed the play at the line.

Lots of Kevin O'Connell apologies needed on this app today

— Minnesota Sports Fan (@realmnsportsfan) November 15, 2026

Questions about O'Connell's play-calling and quarterback coaching have started to surface recently, after the Vikings have struggled on that side of the ball the past two seasons. Even when they were 6-0 earlier this season, the offense ranked 24th in scoring and 29th in net passing.

O'Connell and ownership pushed heavily for the Cousins trade, according to reports. On Sunday, the offense scored three touchdowns, its most since putting up five in Week 1, and its 312 total yards were the most since Week 5. The win moved O'Connell to 50-27 in the regular season.

Lewis called the play O'Connell drew up for the Bartholomew touchdown "filthy". After the game, O'Connell said Cousins "was the one with the ownership of the plan" and ran the show "incredibly clean." He added that it "bodes well for the comfort level hopefully he has with me calling plays."

The Vikings played without Jordan Addison (thumb), Jauan Jennings (calf) and TJ Hockenson (chest/wrist), leaving them thin at receiver. Price caught four passes for 49 yards and scored his first career receiving touchdown, after catching six passes for 32 yards before Sunday. He also returned three kickoffs for 70 yards.

Gavin Bartholomew's touchdown capped an 11-play, 75-yard drive that took 6:50 off the clock in the fourth quarter. Cousins completed eight straight passes on it, the last a 20-yarder to Bartholomew with 8:29 left. Jefferson was on the sideline for that play.

Bartholomew finished with three catches for 36 yards, and Felton had three for 33. As a group, Price, Bartholomew and Felton had 10 catches for 118 yards and two touchdowns, after combining for 16 catches, 127 yards and no touchdowns in the first eight games.

#2702. AFC playoff standings: Browns move up in Week 10 after big Texans win

The Cleveland Browns Week 10 game versus the Houston Texans has huge implications for the AFC playoff picture. Heading into this week, the Browns were tied with the New England Patriots for the final playoff seed in the conference, losing out on the spot due to a tiebreaker, despite losing two straight games.

While we await kickoff in Cleveland's game versus Houston, the Patriots were playing the Detroit Lions over in Germany. Early in the game, both teams lost key players when DE Aidan Hutchinson injured QB Drake Maye during a sack, then injured himself while celebrating that sack.

While the Hutchinson injury is a big deal long term, New England's loss of Maye to an ankle injury all but finished off any hopes they had of securing a victory this week. The Lions ended up controlling the rest of the game, leading to the lopsided 35-10 final score.

With the loss, the Patriots drop to 5-5 and were a half game behind the Browns, who move up to the seventh seed. Cleveland's come-from-behind victory in Week 10 then solidified their seventh seed. As of the end of the early Week 10 games, the AFC playoff standings are as follows:

With a big game on Sunday Night Football (Bengals vs. Steelers) and Monday Night Football with the Baltimore Ravens playing, we will update the AFC playoff picture and AFC North standings early Tuesday morning.

No matter what, the Browns head into their bye week in a playoff spot, with a winning record and three straight home games, where they are undefeated this season.

#2698. NFL Week 10 Winners and Losers: Packers and Lions may be in trouble in NFC North race, leaving Vikings and Bears

Before Sunday, the NFC North looked like it would be a four-team battle royal until the end.

Then a freak injury and a home blowout crystallized the race. We might be down to two teams for the division crown.

The Chicago Bears had the week off and they pushed forward a bit because it's hard to buy the Green Bay Packers and Detroit Lions making much of a move in the division anymore.

The Lions won in Munich early on Sunday but suffered one of the biggest losses of the season when star defensive end Aidan Hutchinson jumped up following a sack to celebrate, and when he came down he suffered a bad knee injury. His "Gramatica" moment is expected to keep him out for a while, via NFL Media. If he's done for the season, the Lions' defense is unlikely to keep the team afloat in the division race.

Detroit is 7-2 and it seems like the season is headed downhill. A defense that was already suspect has to go forward without Hutchinson and linebacker Jack Campbell, who is on injured reserve with a neck injury. It's especially rough that Hutchinson's season might have ended in such a fluky and unnecessary way.

For the Packers, their hot streak came to an abrupt end. The Minnesota Vikings, with Kirk Cousins becoming the first quarterback since 1950 to start for two different teams in back-to-back weeks according to Fox, blew out the Packers 30-10. The Vikings' defense was stifling yet again, including five sacks in the first half and eight overall. Jordan Love looked like a legitimate MVP candidate before Sunday and now he should fall back to the second tier in that race. The Packers (6-4) are just a game-and-a-half behind the Vikings (7-2) in the standings and there's a chance they could regain momentum when Micah Parsons returns to the lineup, but Sunday's home loss was a huge blow to their chances.

The Lions' major injury and Packers' defeat affected more than the NFC North race. NFC wild-card contenders like the Seahawks and Rams were paying attention too. It's not like the Packers and Lions are dead yet, but their paths to the playoffs got significantly tougher on Sunday.

Aidan Hutchinson of Detroit Lions goes down with a knee injury after celebrating getting a sack.

Perhaps we're already down to the Vikings and Bears in the race. The Vikings looked reborn with Cousins providing competent quarterback play five days after Minnesota's big trade with the Raiders. The Bears' offense has been fine all season and their trade for Jeffery Simmons could transform Chicago's defense in the same way Cousins can lift the Vikings' offense.

The race to win the best division in football got some clarity Sunday. It just wasn't good news in Detroit or Green Bay.

Here are the rest of the winners and losers from Week 10 of the NFL season:

Patrick Mahomes and Emmett Johnson: Without Kenneth Walker III on Sunday because of a groin injury, the Chiefs found out some things about their offense.

The first thing they learned was that Johnson, a rookie fifth-round draft pick, took the positive things in a limited role behind Walker and showed he's a legit impact player in his own right. Johnson had a monster game for the Chiefs in a 35-13 win over the Atlanta Falcons. Johnson had 171 total yards and two receiving touchdowns.

The Chiefs know Mahomes is a star but it was nice to see him have his biggest game of the season. Without Walker, Mahomes threw for 348 yards and five touchdowns. It was Mahomes' first five-touchdown game since Week 1 of the 2022 season. He found a new weapon in Josh Downs. The Chiefs acquired him in a trade with the Colts on Tuesday and he scored a touchdown Sunday. He could be a nice addition for Kansas City.

The Chiefs are 7-2, and the way they won Sunday without Walker had to be satisfying.

Chuba Hubbard: He has had a very good season for the Carolina Panthers and it got better Sunday.

Hubbard had a career high with 172 total yards from scrimmage and came through when the Panthers needed it most. Trailing 14-10, Hubbard had a 34-yard catch to set up his 1-yard touchdown run with 1:49 left to give Carolina the lead. After the Saints tied it with a field goal, the Panthers got a quick big play on a pass interference call on a deep pass to Jalen Coker, setting up their own field-goal attempt. Ryan Fitzgerald nailed it from 54 yards as time expired and the Panthers won 20-17.

The Panthers are just 4-5 but right in the mix for the NFC South title. Hubbard has been a big part of those Panthers wins, and he had his best game Sunday.

Post-deadline Jacksonville Jaguars: The Jaguars knew they were in good shape to beat the Tennessee Titans on Sunday. So they decided to give big deadline acquisitions defensive tackle Jordan Davis and safety Jessie Bates III the week off to help rest and acclimate to their new team.

That gamble worked. The Jaguars won without those two defensive standouts, beating the Titans 40-23. Trevor Lawrence was fantastic with 352 yards and three touchdowns. The defense wasn't great, but it will get Davis and Bates in the lineup next week vs. the Giants and will improve immediately. The defense has been pretty good before Sunday too.

The Jaguars are a complete team and a Super Bowl contender. And it should get only better from here.

DeMeco Ryans and the Houston Texans' defense: Houston was supposed to have one of the best defenses in the NFL this season. It hasn't been, and on Sunday it was a big reason the Texans took a loss that is a huge blow to their playoff hopes.

The Texans allowed a game-winning drive to the Cleveland Browns in the final two minutes, with Quinshon Judkins scoring the go-ahead touchdown with 53 seconds left on a 5-yard run. The Browns' defense, which was excellent in the fourth quarter, held after that to clinch the 27-24 win.

The Texans were without a few key players because of injuries. They will argue, rightfully, that a huge unnecessary roughness penalty on cornerback Kwame Lassiter was just a defensive back going for the ball and the hit on the intended receiver wasn't worthy of a flag. It doesn't matter. The Texans are 3-6 and surprisingly, Ryans' defense is a major reason for that disappointing record.

Geno Smith: It has been a rough season for the New York Jets and Smith, and for the quarterback it got even worse.

Smith took a massive hit against the Buffalo Bills and even though he was cleared in the concussion protocol he was later ruled out with an ankle injury. The Jets had been playing well but Cade Klubnik couldn't keep the team in the game and New York lost to the Buffalo Bills 31-12.

The Jets (2-8) have won only once since Week 1. Smith, who was a short-term answer at quarterback anyway, could get pushed out of the starting spot for the rookie Klubnik soon, if it doesn't happen next week against the Chargers.

It's not like winning games is a high priority for the Jets the rest of the season. Smith got injured Sunday and the news might continue to get worse for him.

Kyle McCord: He did the best he could. Even against the Indianapolis Colts, it was asking a lot for a 2025 sixth-round draft pick taking his first NFL snaps to run a competent offense given Miami's supporting cast.

McCord helped the Miami Dolphins stay in the game. But trailing by two points late, he took a third-down sack with the Dolphins just outside of field-goal range. His fourth-down pass was batted down and the Colts survived with a 24-22 win. McCord was better than Daniel Jones, who was awful again. The Dolphins QB had 209 passing yards, almost double the 108 that Jones put up. He also had a rushing touchdown. But McCord couldn't get the play he needed at the end, and his fumble that was recovered in the end zone for a Colts touchdown was a big difference in the final.

McCord will get more chances with Malik Willis on injured reserve for at least three more games. His first effort wasn't bad, it just wasn't good enough for the win.

NFL fans in Germany: The NFL tried to give its German fans a good matchup. The New England Patriots vs. the Detroit Lions was a fun one, until one sack at the end of the Patriots' first drive.

Quarterback Drake Maye suffered an ankle injury on a sack (which was a borderline hip drop tackle) by Lions defensive end Aidan Hutchinson, then Hutchinson suffered a knee injury celebrating. That took out two of the biggest stars in the game. The Patriots' offense, with Tommy DeVito at quarterback, was unwatchable after that. New England, with Maye, had 60 yards on the opening drive and 91 on the 11 drives after that in a 35-10 loss.

There were some things for German fans to cheer. Lions receiver Amon-Ra St. Brown, whose mother is German and carried the country's flag onto the field during pregame introductions, had two fun touchdowns.

Jahmyr Gibbs' brilliance was on full display with 153 yards rushing, 99 yards receiving and two touchdowns. There were some highlights. But it was an ugly game after Maye left it due to injury.

#2697. Russian Teenager Mirra Andreeva Wins WTA Finals; Ukraine’s Elina Svitolina Snubs Handshake

Russian teenager Mirra Andreeva continued her run to a potential No. 1 world ranking by capturing the WTA Finals title on Sunday in Indian Wells.

Andreeva, 19, defeated Elina Svitolina, 7-5, 6-3, in the final. She won the match when Svitolina netted a forehand overhead on match point. It was the Russian's fifth Top 10 win of 2026.

Svitolina, who is Ukrainian, does not shake hands with Russian or Belarusian players due to the war, so Andreeva only shook hands with the umpire following the match during her celebration.

Andreeva will take home $3,370,000 after her 5-0 performance at the event.

Andreeva, the first teenager since Maria Sharapova in 2004 to win the WTA Finals, converted 78% of her firsts serves and won 62% of her first serve points. She was 6-of-16 on break points against Svitolina's serve.

"It was a great week for me," Svitolina said on court. "To be fair, after the first match I wasn't expecting to play here in the final. Really happy to get this chance. Unfortunately it didn't go my way. But I gave it all. I want to say thank. You so much for all the support this week. All the hard work, it was the last match of the season. It motivates me to go to the pre-season and try my best next year."

Andreeva won her maiden Grand Slam title at Roland Garros earlier this year.

Andreeva is currently 293 points behind world No. 1 and 2025 WTA Finals champion Elena Rybakina, who did not play the WTA finals as she rests and recovers from a viral illness.

Andreeva is now on the brink of surpassing her for world No. 1 early in 2027.

"I think it's going to happen very soon," tennis analyst Brad Gilbert said on Tennis Channel this week.

Gilbert also pointed out that if Andreeva -- who is currently ranked No. 3 in the world just behind Aryna Sabalenka, whom Andreeva beat, 6-4, 6-0 in group play – came out of the WTA Finals ranked No. 2 it will help her at the Australian Open in January.

"That puts you on line 128 [of the draw]," Gilbert said. "As the 3 seed, you could be in either one of the 1 or 2's halves. I see it [Andreeva becoming world No. 1] happening very early in 2027."

Rybakina is the defending Australian Open champ, and must defend 2,000 points there, while Andreeva lost in the fourth round in Melbourne last year.

"I think she can go way further than that," Hall of Famer Tracy Austin said on Tennis Channel.

#2696. Mavericks vs Celtics Preview: Closing out a road back-to-back

The Dallas Mavericks (7-6) stumble into Massachusetts on Sunday evening to play the Boston Celtics (10-2) on the second night of a back-to-back. On Saturday evening, the Mavericks elected to play little defense against a Cleveland Cavaliers team resting a big chunk of their rotation and were run up and down the court all evening. Boston comes into this game refreshed, having not played since Wednesday where they held off the Toronto Raptors to close out a four game road stretch.

The Mavericks come into this one needing something but face off against a team that is red hot, winning five in a row and only losing two times this season.

The Celtics come into Saturday's game allowing the fewest points per game to opposing offenses at just 103 a game. While it's true the Celtics have played a pretty weak schedule to date, they're pummeling the teams in front of the on the defensive end.

The Mavericks have looked inept on offense the last few games, turning the ball over often and hitting far too few shots. Given the lack of creation on the offensive end, Dallas is going to need an outlier performance from a good chunk of the roster. If Kyrie Irving happens to sit (he's played on one back-to-back this season), then things get bleak for the Mavericks.

After a three-game homestand where the defense was, at minimum, active, these past two road games against the Timberwolves and the Cavaliers have been nothing short of awful. The Mavericks found themselves down by 15+ points in the first half; in losses, that's a number they've become familiar with.

The transition defense and rebounding have been of the sort to make a coach pull his hair out. Playing against a Boston team that's middle of the pack in terms of points scored but top 6 in efficiency, the Mavericks must be locked in like we haven't seen since the Utah Jazz win.

I say pride in my subheader because at a certain point, energy and effort matter as much as scheme. The Mavericks have looked lethargic on the defensive end with very few bursts. It's awful to watch, they're just not good enough for that to happen in any game.

What in the hell is the NBA league office doing scheduling a road back-to-back where Dallas plays at 6:30 pm CST one night, hops on a plane, then plays the next night at 5:00 pm CST? I don't want to make excuses for guys who get paid a lot to play this game, but this is not fair to the players or the fans.

I don't expect some Mavericks to suit up at all because they tried to come back against the Cavs to no avail. This makes this one a schedule loss (most likely) for everyone involved and that's just stupid.

KFAA Channel 29 or your Mavs TV app will have you covered locally for this one. The radio broadcast will be on KRLD 105.3 The Fan. Tipoff is scheduled for just after 5:00 p.m. CT from the Target Center in Minneapolis, Minnesota.

#2692. Colts’ rookie pays Jonathan Taylor touching compliment in postgame interview

The Indianapolis Colts beat the Miami Dolphins, 24-22, in Week 10. The Colts won the game on the ground with Jonathan Taylor and Seth McGowan combining for 160 yards on 28 carries. In the process Taylor hit a major milestone as he now has over 10,000 scrimmage yards in his career.

The Colts decided to keep Taylor at the trade deadline while they dealt other players like Quenton Nelson, DeForest Buckner, Josh Downs and Keenan Allen. Taylor rewarded them with his solid play on Sunday. Then rookie linebacker CJ Allen explained why Taylor's impact goes well beyond what happens on the field.

Allen, who had nine tackles, was interviewed by CBS's Aditi Kinkhabwala alongside Taylor after the game and revealed just how important the running back is to younger players like him.

MORE: Daniel Jones' reaction to Colts' trade deadline moves says it all

"When I see JT play, he just inspires me because I know how hard he works"CJ Allen explains what Jonathan Taylor means to him and the Colts.🎤: @AKinkhabwala https://t.co/OutLrKVlS8

— NFL on CBS 🏈 (@NFLonCBS) November 15, 2026

"Man, when I see JT play it just inspires me because of how hard he works," Allen told Kinkhabwala before turing to Taylor. "I never told you this, but I watch you, bro. In the locker room and stuff, bro. How you come in. You get your treatment, routine, every single day. I'm just proud of you. I'm glad to be here with you."

Taylor looked genuinely touched. After the trade deadline he said he is happy to go to work and "have my best day each and every single day for my coaches and my teammates." Clearly he's accomplished his goal and at least one of his young teammates is using him as the example he's trying to be.

#2691. Panthers vs Saints: Recap and reaction

The Carolina Panthers overcame an avalanche of penalties, some justified and a lot not, to defeat the New Orleans Saints on a last second field goal, 20-17.

Ryan Fitzgerald started the game out right by kicking the opening kickoff out of bounds. After a few stops, the defense forced the Saints into a 4th and 1. Tyler Shough tried to find Chris Olave, but the pass was tipped and picked off by Devin Lloyd. He ran it all the way back to the New Orleans 23. Chuba Hubbard broke off a nice run to start the drive. The Panthers inched closer to the goal line but ended up in their own fourth and short. Bryce Young tried to scramble out of a collapsing pocket, but he stumbled when trying to make a cut and was touched down just short of the line gain.

The defense took the field with the Saints backed up against their own goal line. They made three straight stops and forced a punt. Trevor Etienne let the punt bounce which added a bunch of extra yardage to it, but he was able to pick it up and get a few of those yards back on the return. The offense rode Chuba Hubbard into Saints territory. The offense picked up a 3rd and 9 on a pass to Darren Waller, but he was called for a phantom offensive pass interference to end the drive. Sam Martin pinned the Saints inside the five with his punt.

The defense had the Saints in a 3rd and 4, but Derrick Brown jumped offsides. It was the team's fifth penalty of the first quarter. It didn't hurt though. The defense got off the field three plays later after Jaelan Philips got a fingertip on Tyler Shough's third down pass.

The Chuba show continued into the second quarter. He caught a check down to pick up a first down into Saints territory then broke a big run down to the 30. On the next play, Bryce Young had Darren Waller wide open down the sideline, but Waller dropped it. McMillan also failed to reel in a catchable ball on third down. Ryan Fitzgerald took the field to miss a field goal and keep the score knotted at zero.

Tyler Shough found Juwan Johnson with a third down pass to get into Panthers territory. On the next play, he and Audric Estime dropped the exchange on a handoff, and Jaelan Phillips scooped it up.

Tommy Tremble took a screen pass into the red zone. A couple plays later, Hubbard ran it in for a touchdown. However, it was called back for a late holding call that also seemed questionable. Dave Canales used a Saints timeout to give the refs a hard time while the broadcast team discussed the second time the officials screwed over Carolina. Young found Waller to eventually pick up the first down. Chuba Hubbard ran it down to the one. Two incompletions set up another nervous fourth down, but Hubbard ran it in, and the officials couldn't find anything to flag.

The Saints picked up a few quick first downs while the broadcast talked about turduckens for an extended period of time. When they stopped talking about turduckens, the defense stuffed a couple of runs. On 4th and inches, they tried a fullback dive, but Lee Hunter broke through the line to stuff the run deep in the backfield.

Young found Tetairoa McMillan to move the offense out to midfield. That's as far as they'd get though. On third down, Young checked it down to a heavily covered Hubbard for no gain. After the punt, Jaelan Phillips got pressure and forced Shough into a sack by Pat Jones. A couple plays later, he got his own sack to end the half with the Panthers up 7-0.

The Panthers got the second half kickoff and immediately got backed up 15 yards because Trevis Gipson tapped a Saints player on the back of the helmet. Dave Canales made a gutsy call to go for it on fourth and short deep in New Orleans territory. It worked, but the offense ultimately had to punt a few plays later anyway.

The Saints picked up a long gain on a diving catch and Devin Lloyd was flagged 15 yards for diving on the un-downed wide receiver. A few plays later, Alvin Kamara checked into the game for his first touch, and he punched it in for a touchdown to tie the game.

The Panthers responded with a field goal drive. Their two biggest gains were a short completion to McMillan and a defensive pass interference on another pass to McMillan. It wasn't much, but it got the offense into long field goal range. Dave Canales trusted his guy, and Ryan Fitzgerald drilled the 55 yard field goal right down the middle.

The Saints went to the air three straight times on their ensuing drive, and the Panthers stopped each one. On third down, Jaelan Phillips collapsed the pocket and forced Shough to step into the waiting arms of Derrick Brown. Feleipe Franks got called for a 15 yard penalty for blocking someone apparently? The drive went nowhere and ended in a sack of Young. It also ended the third quarter.

The defense kept up their strong play and got off the field quickly once again. They forced Shough into a one yard gain on a near sack and forced two incompletions to force a quick three and out. The offense didn't fare any better. They got one first down on a completion to Darren Waller but otherwise didn't do anything and punted the ball right back.

Shough made a couple of completions to move the Saints into Panthers territory. Jaelan Phillips appeared to set the drive back with a huge sack of Shough, but he was called for a horse collar tackle. The Saints moved to the goal line with a pass interference on Corey Thornton on an underthrown ball. Two plays later, Kamara scored his second touchdown of the day to put the Saints up 14-10. It felt like nearly the entire drive was picked up via penalty yards.

Chuba Hubbard broke a couple of tackles on the first play of the ensuing drive and scampered down the sideline for a 29 yard gain. Two plays later, he did the same thing for another 33 yards to get the players down to the one yard line at the two minute warning. Two plays later, Hubbard extended to reach for the goal line. He was ruled short, but the play went to review. It looked pretty clear on replay that the ball was the first thing to touch the ground, and it did so on the goal line, but the call stood somehow. Fortunately, Hubbard was able to punch in the score on the next play.

The Saints got a boost by a defensive face mask in which the defender grabbed the Saint's shoulder pad. That penalty was enough to put the Saints into field goal range, and they made a 55 yarder to tie the game.

The Panthers had 41 seconds to manufacture a game winning drive. In a bit of poetic justice, the Saints were called for a defensive pass interference on a heave from Bryce Young to Jalen Coker. That was enough to get the Panthers into field goal range. Ryan Fitzgerald drilled the 54 yard field goal as time expired.

It's normally pretty lame to complain about the officiating, but this game was an exception. The Panthers out-penalitied the Saints 17 to 3 and out-penalty yardaged the Saints 152 to 46. A number of the calls were questionable to outright bad, and the vast majority of those were significant, game altering penalties. There was an offensive pass interference, offensive holding, two personal fouls, and horse collar tackle that all could easily be argued were very incorrect. They also incorrectly rule Hubbard short of the goal line before he ultimately scored his second touchdown of the game. I don't know if it had the same effect on the players, but as a spectator, it felt like the Panthers had to walk on eggshells to avoid flags, and it's hard to play football that way.

On the football side of things, the defense looked really, really good under Todd Wash. They lived in the backfield on both run and pass plays. Tyler Shough was the Saints leading rusher, and a lot of that was him getting flushed out by defensive pressure. The offense wasn't as impressive, other than Chuba Hubbard. He was a monster. Love that guy.

The Panthers move back into sole possession of the lead in the NFC South. They'll try to add to that with a win over the hobbled Ravens in Charlotte next week.

#2690. Raiders lead Seahawks 10-7 at halftime

It didn’t take long for Fernando Mendoza to show that he brings a different skill set to the Raiders offense than Kirk Cousins.

Mendoza capped the team’s opening drive by scrambling 10 yards for a touchdown and that kind of escapability is not something that Cousins brings to the table at this point in his career. The rookie’s score staked the Raiders to an early 7-0 lead and he picked up 12 more yards on four other runs during the first half of his first NFL start.

One of them was a seven-yard gain that helped set up a field goal that gave the Raiders a 10-7 lead that they took into halftime against the Seahawks. Mendoza also went 11-of-13 for 90 yards as a passer and his legs could not help him avoid a pair of sacks by the Seattle defense.

Seahawks quarterback Drew Lock lost a fumble on the team’s second offensive play, but recovered to produce a touchdown pass to Cooper Kupp that came after he evaded the Raiders rush long enough for the wideout to break open. Lock is 4-of-5 for 62 yards, but the Seahawks only ran 17 offensive plays to 35 for the Raiders over the first 30 minutes.

Both teams picked up significant injuries during the first half. Raiders tight end Brock Bowers has been in and out with an ankle injury and he returned to the locker room during the second quarter. Seahawks right tackle Abe Lucas (ankle) and defensive tackle Jarran Reed (toe) are both considered doubtful to return.
